Four spines and ten map items. ❗ Spines 1 to 3 are the core owner's own assets reproduced and worked; Spine 4 is built here for the one Mains demand this topic owns; and the map set is the minimum a candidate should be able to produce without reference.

### 1. Directive decoding — the owner's table, extended

✅ The core owner's decoding table is the starting point and is reproduced in full, with four rows added by this edition for demands the file has since developed.

| ✅ If the question says | ✅ It is really asking for | ✅ Do **not** |
|---|---|---|
| "How does black cotton soil form?" | Basalt → semi-arid seasonal weathering → clay-rich swelling soil → cotton suitability | Say "it is black because it is fertile" |
| "Discuss the declining groundwater of the Gangetic valley and its effect on food security" | Recharge-versus-withdrawal imbalance, the cropping pattern that drives it, and the transmission to output and equity | Write a generic water-conservation list |
| "Examine mass movements as a hazard" | Slope, water, material and trigger, plus the human amplifiers | List landslide types only |
| "Distinguish weathering from erosion" | In-situ breakdown versus removal and transport | Blur the two |
| "Assess soil degradation in India" | Named degradation pathways, their geography, and conservation logic matched to each | Offer an undifferentiated "way forward" list |
| ⚠️ **"How can it be made effective?"** | ⚠️ **The conditions of success, not the inventory of techniques** — aquifer appropriateness, sizing, maintenance, contamination control, monitoring and demand matching | ⚠️ Spend the answer listing structures |
| ⚠️ **"Compare landslide causes in two regions"** | ⚠️ **The same force balance and the same four causal classes, with different inputs named** — material history, water input, dominant human factor | ⚠️ Write two separate descriptions with no shared frame |
| ⚠️ **"Critically examine" a hazard attribution** | ⚠️ **The preparatory-against-triggering separation**, and the test that a trigger explains *when* but not *why it was able to fail* | ⚠️ Accept the stated cause and elaborate it |
| ⚠️ **Any question quoting a groundwater percentage** | ⚠️ **Agency, assessment year, methodology and scale**, and whether the figure can diagnose the unit it is being applied to | ⚠️ Treat two similar percentages from different compilations as a trend |

### 2. The four evidence units

✅ The core owner supplies three **claim-evidence-significance-limitation** units, reproduced below in its own structure; ⚠️ a fourth is added here for the file's integrating cascade.

> ✅ **Claim:** parent rock can dominate soil character even against the climatic trend. **Evidence:** the Deccan basalt weathers to a clay-rich black soil that retains moisture and self-mulches by cracking, sustaining rain-fed cotton in a semi-arid belt. **Significance:** a single lithological accident can create a national crop region. **Limitation:** the same soil is nitrogen- and humus-poor with a very narrow workable moisture window, so its advantage is specific rather than general.

> ✅ **Claim:** luxuriant vegetation is not evidence of fertile soil. **Evidence:** equatorial rainforest stands on heavily leached, nutrient-poor soils, with the nutrient capital held in the biomass and cycled rapidly. **Significance:** it explains long fallows in shifting cultivation and the rapid yield collapse after clearance. **Limitation:** volcanic and alluvial tropical soils are genuine exceptions, so the generalisation must be stated as a leaching relationship rather than a blanket rule about the tropics.

> ✅ **Claim:** irrigation can degrade the very land it makes productive. **Evidence:** canal command areas without adequate drainage develop waterlogging and surface salt accumulation through capillary rise. **Significance:** it reframes salinity as an engineering-and-governance failure rather than a natural hazard. **Limitation:** reclamation is technically well understood — drainage, gypsum, leaching — so the binding constraint is cost and collective action, not knowledge.

> ⚠️ **Claim (added for this edition):** weathering, erosion, slope failure and groundwater are one system, not four. **Evidence:** in hard-rock India *"the near surface, weathered mantle forms the all important ground water reservoir"*; two independent books list the lowering of the water table and the increase in landslide incidence among the effects of soil erosion; and contour bunding *"also increased the water recharging capacity of wells in the adjoining areas"*, while tubewell vertical drainage reclaimed waterlogged saline land by acting on the water table. **Significance:** it means that soil conservation is groundwater policy, that erosion control is landslide mitigation, and that the weathering history of a terrain determines its aquifer. **Limitation:** none of the three couplings is quantified in any available source — the direction is documented and the magnitude is not — so the argument must be made structurally rather than numerically.

### 3. The nine routing scripts

❗ This topic's structural fact is that **four owned objective demands and exactly one owned Mains demand sit alongside nine demands owned elsewhere whose physical half this file carries**. ⚠️ The skill that converts that into marks is **routing inside an answer**: supplying the mechanism, naming the owner, and stopping. ❗ Nine scripts follow, each in the form a candidate can write.

| # | Demand | 🔁 Correct owner | ⚠️ The script to write |
|---:|---|---|---|
| **1** | **2024 General Studies Paper I** — Gangetic-valley groundwater decline and food security | **Agricultural regions and food security owner** | *"The hydrological mechanism is an alluvial aquifer in which dry-season abstraction by dense tube-well irrigation exceeds recharge, raising pumping depth and reducing well yields. The transmission of that constraint into national foodgrain availability and into the distribution of burden among farm sizes is developed in the agricultural-geography treatment."* ❗ **A declared bounded bridge: Spine 1 lives in this topic's owner and is reproduced above.** |
| **2** | **2025 General Studies Paper III** — seawater intrusion into coastal aquifers | **Environment and ecology owner** | *"The hydraulics are a density-stratified system: fresh water floats on saline water with an interface held by the freshwater head, so over-pumping or reduced recharge lowers that head and the interface advances landward and upward. The coastal-management and regulatory response is developed in the environmental treatment."* |
| **3** | **2025 General Studies Paper III** — depleting groundwater and government steps | **Economy owner** | *"The depletion mechanism is a cropping pattern not matched to rainfall in surface-water-deficient states, met by tube-well abstraction exceeding replenishment. The policy instruments — pricing, procurement, regulation and programme design — are developed in the economic treatment."* ❗ **Teach why; never what the state does.** |
| **4** | **2019 General Studies Paper III** — landslide hazard zonation mapping | **Disaster-management owner** | *"Zonation classifies the propensity of the ground to fail, using material, slope, structure, drainage and cover validated against an inventory. It is susceptibility, not prediction, and carries no time dimension. The national programme and its institutional architecture are developed in the disaster-management treatment."* |
| **5** | **2021 General Studies Paper III** — landslide causes and the national strategy | **Disaster-management owner** | *"Causation follows a four-class checklist — ground, morphological, physical and man-made — in which most contributions are preparatory and only some are triggering, so naming a trigger explains when but not why. The National Landslide Risk Management Strategy is developed in the disaster-management treatment."* ❗ **The strategy is routed entirely.** |
| **6** | **2021 General Studies Paper I** — Himalayan against Western Ghats landslide causes | **Disaster-management owner** | *"The physics is identical and the inputs differ: mechanically shattered rock and old debris on a tectonically active, snow-fed slope against deep chemically weathered regolith on a stable, intensely rain-fed one. The comparative risk-management framing is developed in the disaster-management treatment."* |
| **7** | **2020 General Studies Paper I** — desertification without climatic boundaries | **Environment and ecology owner** | *"Wind erosion operates by suspension, saltation and surface creep wherever soil is dry, weakly aggregated and bare; one documented implication is a lowering of the water table. Desertification as a process, a classification and a treaty obligation is developed in the environmental treatment."* ❗ **The water-table line only.** |
| **8** | **2026 General Studies Paper I** — aeolian processes in desertification | **Arid-processes topic, with the environment owner in support** | *"The transport modes and the desert-pavement residue are set out in the wind-erosion treatment of soil degradation; the aeolian-landform and desertification argument belongs to the arid-processes treatment."* |
| **9** | **2026 General Studies Paper III** — infrastructure against disaster risk reduction in ecologically sensitive areas | **Disaster-management owner** | *"One kilometre of hill road requires the removal of forty to eighty thousand cubic metres of debris, which is simultaneously a toe excavation, an unengineered fill and a drainage discontinuity; and unplanned development, felling and urbanisation are the named causes of Himalayan instability. The infrastructure-against-risk policy argument is developed in the disaster-management treatment."* |

⚠️ **Three further objective demands are routed without any mechanism being supplied**, and they are listed so that no candidate wastes an answer on them: the **2018 Prelims demand on soil organic matter, sulphur and salinisation** is 🔁 owned by the environment owner; the **2018 Prelims demand on sand mining and groundwater** is 🔁 owned by the environment owner and this file supplies **nothing**; the **2021 Prelims demand on the phosphorus cycle and rock weathering** is 🔁 owned by the environment owner, with the chemical-weathering half available if needed; and the **2021 Prelims demand on the global distribution of water** is 🔁 owned by the hydrological-cycle topic.

### 4. The consolidated trap register

⚠️ Every trap in the file, in one place, with its home lesson. ❗ Each appears twice by design — once at the point of teaching and once here.

| # | ❌ The error | ❗ The correction | Lesson |
|---:|---|---|---|
| T1 | Weathering and erosion used interchangeably | In-situ breakdown against removal and transport; **mass movement is neither** | **L1** |
| T2 | "Denudation" used as a synonym for erosion | Denudation is **all four phases**; erosion is one of them | **L1** |
| T3 | Cold treated as the frost-weathering variable | **Repeated crossing of 0 °C with water available** and a rock that admits it | **L2** |
| T4 | Water said to expand by one-tenth on freezing | **About 9 per cent**; the older figure is a dated anchor, attributed | **L2** |
| T5 | ❌ "Carbonation turns limestone into liquid" | ❗ **The rock does not melt**; carbonate is removed **in solution** as ions | **L3** |
| T6 | Three chemical processes given because one book gives three | **Four** — oxidation, carbonation, solution, hydrolysis; the three-fold scheme is **older and narrower** | **L3** |
| T7 | Hydration classified without flagging the dispute | One book calls it physical; the owner and modern treatments call it **chemical** | **L3** |
| T8 | Vegetation assumed to protect rock from weathering | ✅ **A soil cover enhances chemical weathering** — organic acids and CO₂-enriched soil air | **L4** |
| T9 | Regolith thickness read as a weathering rate | Thickness is **production minus removal**; a thin mantle may mean rapid stripping | **L4** |
| T10 | ❌ "Soil is crushed rock" | Soil requires **horizon differentiation**; last season's alluvium is fertile but **immature** | **L5** |
| T11 | Hot humid soils assumed humus-rich | **Decay is very fast**; the nutrient capital is in the **biomass** | **L5, L6** |
| T12 | Regur called a chernozem without qualification | Chernozem is black from **humus**; regur from **basalt-inherited minerals**, and is **poor in nitrogen and humus** | **L6** |
| T13 | Soil Taxonomy's orders and world percentages recited | Use the **design principle** — diagnostic horizons, measurable properties | **L6** |
| T14 | ❌ Black soil attributed to black minerals or to alluvium | ❗ **Basaltic lava**; cotton suitability = **moisture retention + self-mulching cracking** | **L7** |
| T15 | ❌ Laterite treated as a perpetually-wet-climate soil | ❗ **Wet-and-dry alternation**; the dry phase hardens the crust | **L3, L7** |
| T16 | The two desert-soil area figures averaged | **Both reported with both sources**; the reporting bases differ and are unstated | **L7** |
| T17 | A national soil-loss or degraded-land figure quoted | ❗ **Excluded** — the book figures are **mutually contradictory on one page** and partly unit-incoherent | **L8** |
| T18 | Erosion attributed to total rainfall | ✅ **Not total but how and when it comes**; one downpour does what a season cannot | **L8** |
| T19 | Sheet erosion dismissed because it is invisible | ✅ **1 m³/ha/week for 30 years = 15 cm off the whole surface** | **L8** |
| T20 | Table 7.1 attributed to a named source | ❗ **No source line is visible on the page**; report it unattributed | **L8** |
| T21 | Salinity and alkalinity merged | **Chlorides and sulphates** against **sodium carbonate**; **gypsum first** for alkali | **L9** |
| T22 | Leaching prescribed without an outlet | ✅ It **can accumulate saline water downstream** — the salt is **relocated** | **L9** |
| T23 | Salinity treated as a natural arid phenomenon | ✅ **A human-induced degradation pathway**; waterlogging is a chief cause | **L6, L9** |
| T24 | ❌ "Rain lubricated the slope" | ❗ **Pore pressure cuts effective stress and therefore friction**; lubrication is the least of water's three effects | **L10** |
| T25 | Deforestation treated as an erosion issue only | It cuts the **cohesion** term, and the loss is **delayed** by root decay | **L10** |
| T26 | ❌ Soil creep described as rapid or visible | ❗ **Slow and imperceptible**; read it from **tilted trees, fences and posts** | **L11** |
| T27 | "Solifluction" used for any wet flow | ❗ **Strictly periglacial** — saturated regolith over **frozen ground**; use debris or mud flow | **L11** |
| T28 | Subsidence treated as a landslide | **Predominantly vertical**; two mechanisms — **loading** and **abstraction** | **L11, L12** |
| T29 | Rainfall named as the cause of a landslide | ❗ **A trigger explains when, not why it was able to fail**; most contributions are **preparatory** | **L12** |
| T30 | Susceptibility, hazard, vulnerability and risk used loosely | Terrain propensity · plus time · propensity of exposed elements · expected loss | **L12, L13** |
| T31 | A susceptibility map read as a forecast | It carries **no time dimension and no exposure**; forecasting is a different instrument | **L13** |
| T32 | India treated as uniformly landslide-prone | Large parts are recorded as **safe as far as landslides are concerned**; note the **Darjeeling, Karbi Anglong and Assam-plains** exceptions | **L13** |
| T33 | Porosity and permeability treated as one property | ✅ **Clay** is porous and will not yield; ✅ **granite** is **pervious along joints** | **L14** |
| T34 | Vesicular basalt called a good aquifer | **Vesicles are unconnected pores**; the Deccan province is **deficient** | **L14, L16** |
| T35 | ❌ "All wells are artesian" | ❗ A **geometry**, not a depth: confinement, recharge at the edges, sufficient head | **L15** |
| T36 | "Water table" used of a confined aquifer | That is the **piezometric surface** — a pressure statement, possibly above ground | **L14, L15** |
| T37 | Non-closing budget arithmetic reproduced | ❗ **Excluded**; teach the **structure** and the **units problem** — m ha m against BCM | **L15** |
| T38 | The 1971 province scheme used undated | **Lithology has not changed; extraction has** — date the scheme, separate resource from use | **L16** |
| T39 | A national percentage used to diagnose a state | ❗ **National values cannot diagnose one aquifer or district** | **L16, L17** |
| T40 | 61 % (2009) and 60.63 % (2025) compared as a trend | ❗ **Different agency-year, methodology, unit count and definitional base. Do not subtract.** | **L17** |
| T41 | The over-abstraction case written without its counterpoint | ✅ Groundwater irrigation **made the region's productivity and drought resilience possible** | **L17** |
| T42 | "Granary of today, barren land tomorrow" quoted as a prediction | ✅ It is stated **conditionally** — *"if this trend ... continues unabated"* | **L17** |
| T43 | Groundwater problems treated as quantity only | ❌ The owner's trap: **both quantity and quality** are critical | **L17** |
| T44 | A recharge structure recommended without a strata condition | On clay **none of the three designs works**; the specifications name the condition | **L14, L18** |
| T45 | The runoff coefficient of about 0.8 quoted as published | ❗ **It is this edition's inference from the table's own arithmetic**; show the derivation | **L18** |
| T46 | The gallons-per-person harvesting claim quantified | ❗ **No period is stated on the page**; cite qualitatively or omit | **L18** |
| T47 | Techniques listed in answer to "how can it be made effective" | ❗ The directive asks for **conditions of success**, not an inventory | **L18, L19** |

### 5. The cascade, consolidated

⚠️ The file's spine, assembled in one place and stated as a closed loop rather than a chain.

```text
   ================= THE CLOSED LOOP =================

      WEATHERING  (L2-L4)
        makes the REGOLITH
            |                                  \
            |                                   \
            v                                    v
      SOIL (L5-L7)                        THE AQUIFER (L14-L16)
      organised regolith                  "the near surface, weathered
            |                              mantle forms the ALL
            |                              IMPORTANT ground water
            v                              reservoir"
      EROSION (L8)                                ^
      removes the A horizon                       |
            |                                     |
            +--> (3) "Ground water level is ------+   [the cascade,
            |        LOWERED and there is             stated in TWO
            |        decrease in soil moisture"       independent
            |                                         books]
            +--> (7) "The incidence and damaging
            |        power of LANDSLIDES increases"
            |              |
            |              v
            |        SLOPE FAILURE (L10-L13)
            |        -- and erosion/scour is itself
            |           a listed MORPHOLOGICAL CAUSE
            |           in the standard checklist
            |
            v
      CONSERVATION (L9)
      bunding, terracing, afforestation, vertical drainage
            |
            +--> "This has also INCREASED THE WATER RECHARGING
            |     CAPACITY OF WELLS in the adjoining areas"
            |
            +--> afforestation "increases the SEEPAGE of water to
            |     the underground water table"
            |
            +--> vertical drainage RECLAIMED waterlogged saline
            |     land by LOWERING the Punjab-Haryana water table
            |
            +----------> back to THE AQUIFER  ** loop closed **
                              |
                              v
      ARTIFICIAL RECHARGE (L18) is the SAME ACT done deliberately:
      the rural technique list -- gully plug, contour bund,
      percolation tank, check dam -- IS the conservation list
   ===================================================================
   ** THE ONE SENTENCE: in this topic, the structure that conserves
      soil recharges water, the process that strips soil lowers the
      water table and destabilises slopes, and the mantle that
      weathering leaves behind is the reservoir. **
   ===================================================================
```

❗ **One qualification on the whole loop, and it must be stated whenever the loop is used:** ⚠️ **every link above is documented in direction and none is quantified in any source available to this edition.** The books state that bunding increased well recharge and that afforestation increases seepage; **neither gives a volume, a coefficient or a measurement method**. ❗ The argument is therefore **structural**, and an answer that attaches a number to it is inventing one.

### 7. What not to write

❗ Six things this file has deliberately not supplied, which a candidate should therefore not claim.

- ❗ **No national soil-loss or degraded-area figure.** The available book aggregates are mutually contradictory on a single page and partly unit-incoherent. ⚠️ Use the ravine state table and the concentration ratio instead.
- ❗ **No answer letter for any objective demand.** Two official Set-A keys are held in the repository and none is recorded, implied or inferred.
- ❗ **No weathering rate, regolith thickness, cohesion, friction angle or factor of safety for any Indian site.** None is available; the worked stability number in Lesson 10 is explicitly illustrative.
- ❗ **No cause asserted for Malpa, for Malin or for any individual landslide**, and no figure quoted for Joshimath.
- ❗ **No institutional architecture** — no strategy, programme, scheme design, funding line, statutory provision or authority structure from disaster management, environment, economy, agriculture, water or governance. ⚠️ Named and routed only.
- ❗ **No recharge magnitude, coefficient or measured aquifer-scale outcome.** The one coefficient in this file is **derived and tagged as an inference**.

### 8. India hook — the hook inventory itself, used as an instrument

🇮🇳 ⚠️ This lesson's hook is the **inventory in section 6**, used as a working instrument rather than as a list. ❗ The discipline it enforces is the one that most improves an answer: **select the example by the mechanism the question needs.**

⚠️ **Three worked selections.** For a question on **why Indian soils differ**, the Rajmahal boundary is the right hook because it isolates parent material while holding climate nearly constant — the Thar would illustrate climate and answer a different question. For a question on **whether rainfall causes landslides**, Joshimath is the right hook because it is the documented case in which **no single trigger explains the crisis** — Malpa would supply pathos without an attribution argument. For a question on **whether groundwater abundance protects a region**, Punjab is the right hook precisely because it sits on the **richest province in the country** and is nonetheless the most stressed.

❗ **Limitation, stated with the hook.** ⚠️ An example bank is only as good as the **mechanism attached to each entry**; a hook recalled without its mechanism is a place name, and place names earn nothing. ⚠️ Second, ❗ **every figure attached to these hooks carries the provenance stated at its home lesson** — book-era, undated, reported, or derived — and an answer must carry that provenance with it rather than laundering it into a bare fact.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | ⚠️ Owning a demand | ⚠️ Bridging a demand |
|---|---|---|
| Count in this topic | ❗ **Five** — four objective, one Mains | ❗ **Nine** |
| What the answer must contain | **The complete answer**, to full depth | **The mechanism**, then an explicit route **by name** |
| Failure mode if misjudged | Under-depth on an owned demand | **Trespass** — writing another owner's institutional content |
| ❗ The skill | Depth | ❗ **Restraint, exercised visibly** |

**Strongest objection.** Answer craft is padding. A candidate who knows the content will structure an answer naturally, and a lesson on spines and routing scripts substitutes technique for knowledge.

**Reply.** The objection fails on this topic specifically, for a reason visible in its arithmetic. ❗ **Nine of this topic's fourteen examinable demands are owned elsewhere**, so the most common failure here is not ignorance but **misallocation** — writing the disaster-management half of a landslide answer, or the economy half of a groundwater answer, both of which feel like substance and earn nothing in a Geography paper. ⚠️ Restraint of that kind is **not natural**; it has to be built, and it has to be **visible in the script** so that the examiner sees the mechanism supplied and the handover made deliberately. ❗ Equally, the **one owned Mains demand turns on a single word — *effective*** — and a candidate who has not decoded that directive will write an inventory and lose the question while knowing every technique in it.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ **Directive conventions are not published**, and the decoding table above is an inference from how demands have been framed, not a statement of marking practice. ⚠️ Likewise the ownership routings reflect **this repository's current owner assignments**, which are revisable — ❗ the landslide routing in particular supersedes an earlier pointer following a documented re-evaluation. ⚠️ A candidate should therefore treat the scripts as **a discipline for supplying mechanism and marking handover**, not as a claim about where marks are administratively allocated.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** "Important Geophysical phenomena ... geographical features and their location-changes" (General Studies Paper I); **"Disaster and disaster management" as support only** (General Studies Paper III).
- **Verified linkage:** ❗ **all five owned demands consolidated** — 2018 General Studies Paper I on urban water harvesting, and the objective demands of 2021, 2023, 2024 and 2025 — **plus explicit route scripts for all nine bridged demands**. ❗ **No answer letter appears for any objective demand.** ❗ **No demand owned by this topic appears in 2026.**
- **Probable framing:** this lesson is not examined; it is the instrument with which the other eighteen are examined.
- **Traps:** the forty-seven in section 4, of which the most costly in this topic are **trespass into another owner's institutional content** and **quoting an undated or contradicted statistic**.
- **Answer use:** decide the directive first, then the spine, then the two or three hooks whose mechanisms the question needs — and write the route explicitly wherever the demand belongs elsewhere.

Revision notes:

- ✅ **Spine 1, fifteen marks — groundwater and food security, seven steps:** thesis · physical system · imbalance · transmission · ❗ **balance** · response logic (demand, supply, institutional) · graded conclusion. ❗ **A declared bounded bridge to the food-security owner.**
- ✅ **Spine 2, ten marks — soil as hinge:** parent material and climate → pedogenic direction → property → crop and settlement → degradation risk → conservation response. ❗ Qualify: **soil sets probabilities, not destiny**.
- ⚠️ **Spine 3 — slope failure:** setting → force balance → the four preparatory classes → the trigger → why the trigger is not the cause → India geography → physical mitigation mapped to balance terms → ❗ **route the institutional half by name**.
- ⚠️ **Spine 4, fifteen marks — the one owned Mains demand:** frame as **restoration of a destroyed recharge pathway** → definitions → the four potential-area criteria → one design specified → ❗ **the five conditions of effectiveness** → the harvest table with the inferred coefficient → conclude that supply and demand sides are **complements**.
- ⚠️ **"How can it be made effective?" asks for conditions of success, not an inventory.** The single highest-value decoding in this file.
- ✅ **Four evidence units:** parent rock against climate (regur) · luxuriance is not fertility (rainforest) · irrigation degrades what it makes productive (canal salinity) · ⚠️ **and the integrating cascade, added here**.
- 🔁 ❗ **Nine routing scripts:** 2024 GS-I Gangetic groundwater → food-security owner · 2025 GS-III intrusion → environment owner · 2025 GS-III depletion and steps → economy owner · 2019 GS-III zonation → disaster-management owner · 2021 GS-III causes and strategy → disaster-management owner · 2021 GS-I Himalaya against Ghats → disaster-management owner · 2020 GS-I desertification → environment owner · 2026 GS-I aeolian processes → arid-processes topic · 2026 GS-III infrastructure against risk → disaster-management owner.
- ❗ **The removal test for every sentence:** if deleting the institution's name leaves a physical statement intact, it is this topic's; if it leaves nothing, it belongs to another owner.
- ❗ **Ten map items:** Chambal-Yamuna ravine tract (Gwalior, Morena, Bhind; Agra, Etawah, Jalaun) · Shiwalik *chos* (Hoshiarpur) · **the limit of wind erosion, a line** · the north-east shifting-cultivation belt · the eight soil groups · the Very High landslide zone · the eight groundwater provinces · the bhabar-tarai spring line · the Punjab-Haryana-Rajasthan over-extraction belt · the coastal saline-intrusion tracts.
- ❗ **The loop, in one sentence:** the structure that conserves soil recharges water; the process that strips soil lowers the water table and destabilises slopes; and the mantle weathering leaves behind is the reservoir. ⚠️ **Documented in direction, unquantified in magnitude.**
- ❗ **Six things not to write:** no national soil-loss figure · no answer letter · no Indian weathering rate, regolith thickness or stability value · no asserted cause for an individual landslide and no Joshimath figure · no institutional architecture · no recharge magnitude.

### Concept check

**Question:** A question reads: "Landslides in the Himalaya are a consequence of development rather than of geology. Critically examine. (15 marks, 250 words)." Set out how you would allocate the answer, which spine you would use, which hooks you would select and why, and exactly where you would route.

**Model answer:** The directive is **critically examine**, so the answer must take a position and qualify it, not survey. The proposition is **half true**, and the structure should establish which half. **Allocation of roughly 250 words:** about 60 to the geological preparation, about 90 to the developmental contribution, about 50 to the force-balance frame that unites them, about 30 to the route, and about 20 to a graded conclusion. **Spine:** Spine 3, the slope-failure spine — setting, force balance, preparatory classes, trigger, India geography, mitigation, route — compressed because 250 words will not carry all seven stages at length. **The argument:** geology supplies the preparation that the proposition understates. The Himalaya is young and actively deforming, lies in the highest seismic zone, and carries mechanically shattered rock, glacial and old-landslide debris; the standard four-class checklist places weak, sensitive and weathered materials, adverse jointing and permeability variation in the **ground** class, and tectonic uplift and erosion in the **morphological** class, both of which are preparatory and neither of which development created. But development supplies contributions that are both large and recent, and the source names them directly: unplanned and unscientific development activity, indiscriminate felling of trees, and urbanisation. Each maps to a term in the force balance — felling reduces the cohesion that root reinforcement supplies, with the loss delayed by root decay; road construction removes a buttress at the toe, since one kilometre requires the removal of forty to eighty thousand cubic metres of debris, which then loads the slope below; and construction and drainage disruption add weight and raise pore-water pressure. **Hooks:** Joshimath, because it is the documented case in which no single trigger explains the crisis and in which five of six reported contributions are preparatory and four of those are man-made and decades old; and, briefly, the hill-road debris figure, because it quantifies the developmental term. **The route:** one sentence naming the disaster-management treatment as the home of the risk-management and strategy argument, supplied after the mechanism and before the conclusion. **Conclusion, graded:** geology decides where failure is possible and development decides where and how fast the factor of safety is driven towards unity, so the proposition is right about the proximate change and wrong about the sufficient condition.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not read "critically examine" as licence to balance by listing points on both sides. It requires a **verdict with its grounds**, and in this case the verdict is that the two contributions are not alternatives — one supplies the possibility and the other supplies the rate.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "In this subject the hardest skill is knowing what not to write." Discuss, with reference to weathering, erosion, landslides and groundwater. *(Discuss — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The claim holds because much of this subject's examinable material is owned elsewhere. A landslide answer must supply causation — the four-class checklist, the separation of preparatory from triggering factors, the force balance — and must then stop, because risk-management strategy belongs to disaster management. A groundwater answer must supply the budget, the province geography and the cropping driver, then stop, because policy belongs to economics and coastal management to environmental studies. Restraint is also evidential. National soil-loss figures in circulation are contradictory within a single printed page, so the defensible move is to quote the ravine concentration ratio instead. Two percentages from different compilations may not be differenced. No objective answer may be inferred. And no cause may be asserted for an individual landslide without a site investigation. The qualification is that restraint must be visible: an answer should **supply the mechanism and name the route**, not omit.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for two boundaries stated with the owner named and the retained mechanism specified; 3 for two evidential exclusions with their reasons — the contradicted aggregates and the incommensurable percentages; 2 for answer-neutrality and non-attribution of individual events; 1 for the requirement that restraint be visible as an explicit route; 1 for one correct retained mechanism quoted accurately. Deduct 3 for supplying institutional content from another owner, and 2 for quoting an excluded statistic.

# VERIFIED PYQ LINKAGE AND ANSWER APPROACHES

## What this topic owns, and what it does not

This topic directly owns **five verified demands — four objective and exactly one Mains** — while **nine further demands whose physical half it must carry are owned elsewhere**. ❗ That five-against-nine asymmetry is the defining structural fact of the topic, and it determined the weighting of the nineteen lessons: the one owned Mains demand is taught to full answer depth at Lesson 18, the deepest lesson in the file, while each bridged demand receives a mechanism treatment and an explicit, named route.

❗ **No demand owned by this topic appears in 2026.** Every 2026 ledger was read item by item. The General Studies Paper I ledger's twenty questions contain no route to this topic; the General Studies Paper III ledger's nearest demand routes to the disaster-management owner; and the 2026 objective ledger's Geography rows route to the fluvial, vulcanism, crust-and-rocks and lakes-and-wetlands owners. ❗ **That nil return is stated rather than filled, and no 2026 presence is manufactured.**

❗ **No answer letter is recorded or inferred for any objective question below.** That includes the two demands whose official Set-A keys are physically present in the repository. **No truth marking, elimination cue or corrective option mapping appears anywhere in this file.** ❗ **No solved previous-year model answer appears**, and no replacement solved-practice workbook has been created.

## Approach notes — demand by demand

**P1 · 2018 General Studies Paper I Q14 · "How can it be made effective?" · 15 marks · 250 words.** ❗ **The directive is the question.** *How can it be made effective* asks for **conditions of success**, not for an inventory of techniques, and an answer that lists pits, trenches and check dams has answered a different and easier question. Open by **reframing**: urban harvesting is not a supply addition but the **restoration of a recharge pathway that paving destroyed** — ✅ the owner's fourth need item records that infiltration *"has decreased drastically in urban areas because open ground has been paved"*. Define both terms, noting that ✅ **artificial recharge is augmentation "at a rate exceeding that under natural conditions"**, which embeds a measurement obligation. Then the four **potential-area criteria**, of which the second — ✅ **substantially de-saturated aquifers** — is the physical precondition most answers omit. Specify **one** design concretely rather than listing four: ✅ the recharge pit for about 100 square metres of roof, 1 to 2 metres wide and 2 to 3 metres deep, graded with boulders of 5 to 20 centimetres, gravel of 5 to 10 millimetres and coarse sand of 1.5 to 2 millimetres **so that silt deposits on the sand and can be removed**. Then the **five conditions** that constitute effectiveness: aquifer-appropriate, sized to catchment, maintained, protected by first-flush diversion and filtration, monitored, and matched to demand. Attach the demand side — ✅ **12.5 litres per flush against cisterns needing 5 to 7**, and greywater reuse. ❗ Use the **CGWB 2003 harvest table** for a computable claim, and if the runoff coefficient is used, **tag it as an inference from the table's own arithmetic**. ❗ Do **not** expound river interlinking; ❗ do **not** quantify the gallons-per-person claim; ❗ do **not** teach the scheme's governance design.

**P2 · 2021 Prelims Q64.** Mechanism owed: **parent rock and pedogenesis**. ✅ Black or regur soil forms by the **weathering of the Deccan basalt under a semi-arid seasonal climate**; ✅ a second source gives the parent material as **the weathered rocks of Cretaceous lava**. ✅ Its chemistry is **argillaceous with a clay factor of 62 per cent or more, about 10 per cent iron oxide, 6 to 8 per cent alumina, 9 to 10 per cent lime and magnesium carbonates and potash under 0.5 per cent**, with **kankar nodules**. ❗ The distinctions a statement-level question can test: **basaltic lava, not "black minerals" and not alluvium**; **moisture retention plus self-mulching cracking, not chemical richness**; **poor in nitrogen and humus**; and the **"tropical chernozem" label as a resemblance that imports the wrong causation**. ❗ *Answer-neutral; the official key is unavailable locally and nothing is inferred.*

**P3 · 2023 Prelims Q76.** Mechanism owed: **the dating and provenance of national groundwater statistics**. ⚠️ Any share of global withdrawal is **agency-specific, year-specific and methodology-specific**; a **withdrawal** share is not an **extraction-stage** figure and not a **depletion** figure; and India's position in such rankings follows from the **scale of irrigated agriculture**, not from scarcity. ❗ The transferable discipline is the owner's: recharge, extraction stage, assessment-unit category and notified area each require **agency, assessment year, methodology and scale**, and **national values cannot diagnose one aquifer or district**. ❗ *Answer-neutral; key unavailable locally; nothing inferred. No figure from any United Nations report is quoted anywhere in this file.*

**P4 · 2024 Prelims Q7.** Mechanism owed: **rainfall chemistry and chemical weathering**. ⚠️ Rainwater is **naturally mildly acidic** through dissolved atmospheric carbon dioxide, before any pollution; it becomes **more acidic in the soil zone**, because root respiration and microbial decay enrich soil air in carbon dioxide and litter decay supplies organic acids. ❗ Consequently ✅ **a soil cover enhances rather than protects against chemical weathering**, and chemical weathering is a **subsurface process concentrated at the weathering front**. ⚠️ Rainfall **amount** and rainfall **chemistry** are different variables with different effects. ✅ The four reactions are **oxidation, carbonation, solution and hydrolysis**. ❗ *Answer-neutral: the repository holds the official Set-A key and it is deliberately neither recorded nor inferred.*

**P5 · 2025 Prelims Q28.** Mechanism owed: **porosity against permeability**. ✅ **Most porous rocks are also permeable**; ✅ **some are porous but impermeable, clay being the standard case**; ✅ and **granite, though crystalline and effectively non-porous, is often pervious along joints**. ⚠️ Add **specific yield against specific retention** — clay's porosity is almost entirely retention — and the **primary against secondary porosity** distinction on which Lesson 16's integrating argument rests. ❗ *Answer-neutral: the repository holds the official Set-A key and it is deliberately neither recorded nor inferred.*

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

Four checks. Three consolidate an arc; the fourth spans the whole topic, which is justified because the chain from a weathering reaction to a recharge structure cannot be tested inside any single arc.

### Cumulative check A — the weathering and soil arc, Lessons 1 to 9

**Question:** Build the chain from "a rock at the surface is out of equilibrium with it" to "a canal command area in western Uttar Pradesh develops a white crust within a decade", naming every intermediate step, and identify the point at which a natural process becomes a human-induced one.

**Model answer:** One: a rock formed at depth meets oxygen, liquid water and low pressure at the surface, and weathering is its re-equilibration. Two: mechanical processes multiply surface area while chemical reactions — oxidation, carbonation, solution and hydrolysis — change mineralogy, with hydrolysis dominant because silicates dominate the crust. Three: the product is regolith, and because percolating water is loaded with carbon dioxide and organic acids from any soil above, the weathering front at the base of the profile deepens rather than being protected. Four: where organisms, organic matter and water movement organise that regolith, horizon differentiation produces soil, with eluviation removing material from the A and E horizons and illuviation depositing it in the B. Five: the direction of that transfer is set by the balance between percolation and evaporation, so where evaporation dominates the arrows reverse and calcification or salinisation occurs. Six: in India these processes acting on six parent-material classes produce eight soil groups, among them the alluvial soils of the northern plains, which the owner describes as exposed to waterlogging and salinity under canal irrigation. Seven: canal irrigation on flat or saucer-shaped terrain, with seepage from unlined canals, raises the water table. Eight: once the table is near enough for capillary continuity, dry-season evaporation draws water upward through the capillary fringe. Nine: the dissolved salts that the water carried are left at the surface as an efflorescence, named in India as reh, kallar, usar and thur, among others, and affecting about 1.25 million hectares in Uttar Pradesh alone. The process becomes human-induced at step seven. Everything before it would have happened without intervention, at rates set by climate and lithology; step seven **creates both preconditions of the salinisation pathway at once** — a near-surface water table and a continuing evaporative gradient — which is why the owner classes salinisation as a human-induced degradation pathway and not only a natural one, and why waterlogging is correctly described as a chief cause of salinity rather than a parallel problem.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the surface crust as the problem and the chemistry as the explanation. The crust is the terminal deposit of an upward transport system whose engine is the water table and whose pump is evaporation; a remedy that does not act on one of those two is treating the symptom.

### Cumulative check B — the slope arc, Lessons 10 to 13

**Question:** A single storm crosses a district. One slope fails catastrophically, one creeps imperceptibly as it has for decades, one cliff loses a block, and most slopes do nothing. Explain all four outcomes with one framework, and state what the pattern establishes about hazard attribution.

**Model answer:** One framework explains all four: the factor of safety, the ratio of resisting force — cohesion plus a frictional term proportional to effective stress — to driving force, the downslope component of weight. **The catastrophic failure** had a factor of safety already close to unity. Preparatory factors had brought it there: weathered or sensitive material, adverse jointing, perhaps toe excavation or crest loading, lost vegetation and disrupted drainage, all of which act over years or decades. The storm then raised pore-water pressure, which subtracts from the normal force and so reduces friction, and added mass; that last increment carried the ratio below one. **The creeping slope** is not failing at all in the same sense. Creep is slow, near-continuous movement produced by repeated expansion and contraction lifting particles normal to the slope and letting them settle vertically, so each cycle displaces them minutely downhill. It has no failure surface and no event, and its evidence is read from tilted trees, fences and posts rather than from a scar. **The cliff fall** demonstrates that water need not be inside the material at all. Wave or stream undercutting removes support at the base, an overhang develops, tension concentrates at its back where rock is weakest, and a block detaches and leaves the surface entirely — the mechanism recorded for a cliff fall on the Kanara coast. **And most slopes did nothing** because their factors of safety were comfortably above one; the same rainfall was insufficient to move them. **What the pattern establishes** is the central discipline of hazard attribution: the rainfall is a property of the weather, identical across the district, while the outcomes are properties of the slopes. A trigger therefore explains **when** a failure occurred and cannot explain **why that slope was able to fail**, which is why the standard four-class checklist is dominated by preparatory factors in the ground, morphological and man-made classes, and why mitigation acts almost entirely on those — drainage control, toe support, land use and vegetation — rather than on the trigger, which cannot be managed.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not infer causal weight from the magnitude of a trigger. An exceptional storm on a well-prepared slope and an ordinary shower on a slope at limiting equilibrium produce the same outcome, and the reported rainfall cannot distinguish them.

### Cumulative check C — the groundwater arc, Lessons 14 to 18

**Question:** Explain, in one connected argument, why India's richest aquifer is also its most stressed, and why that is not a paradox. Then state what the argument implies about where recharge structures should be placed.

**Model answer:** The argument runs through five established steps. **One, the resource.** The Ganga-Brahmaputra alluvial province is recorded as the richest in the country, with well-defined bhabar, tarai and axial belts, great thicknesses of unconsolidated sorted sediment, continuous recharge from a large river system and a high water table. That combination gives it both high storage and high transmissivity, so wells are cheap, reliable and widely distributed. **Two, the setting.** The north-western states drawing on its western margin receive under forty centimetres of annual rainfall and are deficient in surface water, so groundwater is the primary irrigation source rather than a supplement. **Three, the demand.** Demand rose with the Green Revolution from the 1960s and was then raised further by a cropping shift: the region is climatically more suited to wheat, and the preference for rice fixed water demand above replenishment before any efficiency measure could act. **Four, the accounting.** In budget terms, disposal item four — artificial discharge by pumping — grew until total disposal exceeded total supply, and the deficit is met from storage. The sources record these states as exploiting more than a hundred per cent of the available groundwater, and the current Central Ground Water Board assessment places over-exploited assessment units in exactly this north-western cluster. **Five, the resolution of the apparent paradox.** Abundance did not cause the depletion; it **permitted** it. A thick, reliable, easily accessible resource lowers the cost of abstraction, supports a cropping pattern that rainfall could never support, and — decisively — **masks over-draft for years**, because great storage means that falling levels appear slowly and deepening wells continues to work for a time. A poor aquifer would have failed early and limited the demand it could support; a rich one permitted the mismatch to grow until it was structural. **What this implies for recharge placement** is a matching rule rather than a universal prescription. Structures must go where the formation is **de-saturated and permeable**, which is precisely one of the four potential-area criteria. In the alluvial province that condition is satisfied over large areas and the constraint is scale rather than suitability. In the crystalline province, by contrast, the reservoir is the weathered mantle and the conduit is the fracture system, so structures must be sited on weathered or fractured zones and sized to small catchments. On clay, no infiltration structure works at all. The conclusion is that recharge is a **local hydrogeological decision**, which is also why management at gram-panchayat and block scale, using locally prepared water budgets, is the structurally correct scale.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat resource richness as protection. Storage buys time and conceals the trend, so the richest aquifers are the ones in which over-draft can persist longest before it becomes visible.

### Cumulative check D — the whole topic, Lessons 1 to 19

**Question:** State what this topic establishes about the relationship between a physical process and a human outcome, drawing on at least five lessons, and say precisely where the relationship stops holding.

**Model answer:** The topic's recurring finding is that **one physical process generates several human outcomes, and that the outcomes diverge as human decisions accumulate between the process and the result**. It begins tightly coupled. Weathering produces regolith, and what happens to that regolith is decided by relief alone: stripped on a scarp, organised into soil on an interfluve, left in place as an aquifer beneath both. In hard-rock India the near-surface weathered mantle is the all-important groundwater reservoir and the route to the underlying fracture systems, so a chemical reaction between water and feldspar is, without any intervening decision, the reason half the country has water at all (L3, L4, L16). The coupling remains tight through soil: parent material and climate fix the pedogenic direction, which fixes properties such as moisture retention and nutrient status, which fix crop association — the Deccan basalt weathering to a clay-rich soil with a clay fraction above sixty-two per cent that self-mulches by cracking, and so sustains rain-fed cotton in a semi-arid belt (L6, L7). The chain then loosens, and it loosens through decisions. Erosion's effects list runs from lost topsoil through lowered water tables to increased landslide incidence — a single process with three outcomes — but which of them dominates depends on land use, which is itself listed among the six controls on weathering and among the four classes of landslide cause (L4, L8, L12). By the slope lessons, the physics specifies only possibility: the factor of safety says where failure can occur, while felling, road cutting, crest loading and drainage disruption decide how fast it is driven towards unity, and settlement position decides whether anyone dies — Malpa and Malin were lethal because of where the villages stood, not because the events were exceptional (L10, L12, L13). By the groundwater lessons the physics has become almost silent on outcome: the same tubewell reclaimed waterlogged saline land in the Punjab-Haryana plain and then became the instrument of over-abstraction in the same region, with the device unchanged and only the target state of the water table different (L9, L17). **The relationship stops holding at the point where the outcome depends on a choice that the physics does not constrain.** Physical geography can state where deep regolith will form, which soils will occur on which rocks, which slopes can fail, which provinces can store water, and which formations will accept recharge. It cannot state when a slope will fail, how much a failure will cost, which crop a farmer will plant, or whether a recharge structure will be maintained. Two of this topic's most important conclusions are consequences of that limit: a susceptibility map is not a forecast, because the physics supplies propensity and not timing; and "how can water harvesting be made effective" is answered not by physics but by conditions — aquifer appropriateness, sizing, maintenance, contamination control, monitoring and demand matching — of which only the first is a physical question at all.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not conclude from the loosening of the chain that physical geography is merely background. The physics fixes the **set of possible outcomes** and rules out whole classes of response — deepening wells in crystalline terrain, recharge pits on clay, leaching alkali land without gypsum — and ruling out the impossible is the discipline's most useful contribution to a policy argument.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

⚠️ These are **original practice prompts written for this edition**. They are not previous-year questions and must not be cited as such. None duplicates a lesson-local practice question. Every rubric is specific to its own answer.

## 10-mark practice (about 150 words)

**Practice question 1 [10] — one process, three products.** "Weathering has three products, and only one of them is sediment." Explain. *(Explain — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** Weathering is in-situ disintegration and decomposition, producing regolith — the mantle of weathered waste lying on its parent rock. What becomes of that mantle depends on relief. On a steep slope it is stripped, entering erosion, transportation and deposition as sediment. On a stable interfluve it is organised by organic matter, biological activity and water movement into horizons, and becomes soil; that is the second product, and it is the hinge between physical and economic geography, since deep soils of the Ganga plain and the Kerala coast support dense populations while shallow coarse soils of Telangana and Rajasthan support few. Beneath both it is the third product: in hard-rock India the near-surface weathered mantle is the all-important groundwater reservoir and the route by which water circulates through underlying fracture systems. Over half the country's area therefore depends for its water on the fact that its rock has rotted.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for regolith defined as the in-situ product with the owner's or book's wording; 3 for all three destinations named — sediment, soil, aquifer; 2 for relief identified as the variable that selects between them; 1 for the weathered-mantle reservoir statement attributed; 1 for one Indian consequence. Deduct 2 for treating weathering only as preparation for erosion.

**Practice question 2 [10] — a measurement discipline.** "Every groundwater percentage is a statement about a method." Discuss. *(Discuss — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The proposition is defensible, and Indian data demonstrate it. Groundwater assessment has a documented lineage: Khosla in 1949 estimated annual run-off at 167.4 million hectare metres; the National Commission on Agriculture in 1976 put total groundwater at 67 million hectare metres with 35 usable; a scientific basis began in 1979 with a committee constituted by the Agriculture Refinance and Development Corporation; and the Ground Water Estimation Committee of 1984 set the recorded norms. Each figure rests on a different definition, so none is comparable with another. The lesson sharpens at the recent end: a level of development of 61 per cent for 2009 and a stage of extraction of 60.63 per cent in the Central Ground Water Board's 2025 compilation look almost identical, yet differ in agency-year, methodology, assessment-unit count and definitional base. The discipline follows: attach agency, assessment year, methodology and scale, and never difference two compilations.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for three stages of the assessment lineage with their dates; 3 for the 2009-against-2025 pair presented as incommensurable with at least two reasons; 2 for the four-part dating discipline stated; 1 for correct unit handling between million hectare metres and billion cubic metres; 1 for naming the issuing body of the current compilation. Deduct 3 for differencing the two percentages, and 2 for any undated figure.

## 15-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 3 [15] — the integrating claim.** "Soil conservation in India should be evaluated as water policy." Critically examine. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The claim is strongly supported in direction and weakly supported in magnitude. **The case for it.** Two independently authored books list, among the effects of soil erosion, both a lowered groundwater level with reduced soil moisture and an increase in the incidence and damaging power of landslides. The mechanism is infiltration: stripping the topsoil and sealing the surface by raindrop impact raises runoff and reduces percolation, so erosion is a recharge problem before it is a fertility problem. Conservation reverses the chain. Contour bunding, besides raising crop yields by about twenty-five per cent across six states, is recorded as having **also increased the water recharging capacity of wells in adjoining areas**; afforestation reduces runoff intensity and **increases the seepage of water to the underground water table**; and vertical drainage by tubewell acted directly on the water table to reclaim waterlogged saline land in the Punjab-Haryana plain. The technique lists confirm it: the rural rainwater-harvesting set — gully plug, contour bund, percolation tank, check dam — is the soil-conservation set renamed. **The limits.** No source quantifies the recharge gain; the direction is documented and the magnitude is not. Surface detention need not recharge a deep or confined aquifer. And conservation acts on the supply side of a deficit that, in the north-west, is created by a cropping pattern. The verdict is that conservation is **necessary water policy and insufficient water policy**, and should be evaluated as one instrument among several rather than as a substitute for demand management.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 3 for the erosion-to-water-table link evidenced from the effects lists with the infiltration mechanism supplied; 3 for two conservation-to-recharge couplings quoted or closely paraphrased with their recorded yield data; 3 for the identity between the rural recharge set and the conservation set; 3 for three limits, including the absence of any quantification and the confined-aquifer caveat; 2 for the demand-side qualification; 1 for a graded verdict. Deduct 3 for asserting a recharge volume or coefficient, and 2 for treating conservation purely as an agricultural measure.

**Practice question 4 [15] — attribution under uncertainty.** "In slope hazards, the honest answer is usually that the cause is plural." Discuss, with Indian illustration. *(Discuss — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** Plurality is not a hedge here; it is what the standard framework asserts. The checklist of landslide causes in the national disaster-management manual of 2001 has four classes. **Ground causes** — weak, sensitive or weathered materials, adverse structure such as joints and fissures, variation in permeability and plasticity. **Morphological causes** — tectonic uplift, wind or water erosion, scour, deposition loading the slope crest, vegetation removal by fire or drought. **Physical causes** — prolonged precipitation, rapid draw-down, earthquake, thawing, shrink and swell, artesian pressure. **Man-made causes** — excavation especially at the slope toe, crest loading, reservoir draw-down, deforestation, irrigation, mining, vibration, and leakage from utilities. Most are **preparatory**, acting over years to decades to drive the factor of safety towards unity; only some are **triggering**, supplying the last increment. Naming a trigger therefore explains when, not why the slope was able to fail — which is why identical rainfall leaves most slopes standing. Joshimath is the documented Indian case. Reported expert findings converge on an old landslide and morainic debris foundation, internal erosion from subsurface seepage, drainage blocked and diverted by construction, loading beyond bearing capacity, wastewater discharge and a seismically active setting; the advanced owner's summary is that **no single trigger explains the whole crisis**. Five of those six contributions are preparatory and four are man-made and decades old. The qualification is that the checklist does not weight its items, so it establishes what must be managed without apportioning responsibility — which remains a site-investigation question.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 4 for all four causal classes named with at least two items each and the source dated; 4 for the preparatory-against-triggering separation with the factor-of-safety logic; 3 for the Indian case worked as a multi-causal attribution rather than described; 2 for the discrimination argument that identical rainfall leaves most slopes standing; 1 for the no-weighting qualification; 1 for routing the institutional half. Deduct 3 for naming a single cause, and 2 for quoting an unverified figure for the Indian case.

## 20-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 5 [20] — the full integration argument with its counter-case.** "Weathering, soil erosion, mass movement and groundwater are one system in India, not four subjects." Critically examine. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The claim is evidenced rather than stylistic, and three couplings carry it. **First**, in hard-rock terrain the near-surface weathered mantle is described as the all-important groundwater reservoir and the source of circulation through underlying fracture systems; since over half the country's area is Pre-Cambrian crystalline with no primary porosity, weathering literally manufactures the aquifer. **Second**, two independently authored books list, among the effects of soil erosion, both a lowered water table with reduced soil moisture and an increased incidence and damaging power of landslides; and the standard four-class landslide checklist independently places wind and water erosion and scour among morphological causes. **Third**, the response runs back: contour bunding raised yields by about twenty-five per cent and also increased the water recharging capacity of neighbouring wells; afforestation increases seepage to the water table; and tubewell vertical drainage reclaimed waterlogged saline land by acting on the table itself. The rural recharge technique list is the conservation list renamed. **Three limits must be conceded.** None of the three couplings is quantified in any available source, so the argument is structural, not numerical. The couplings are regional: they are strongest in hard-rock and semi-arid India and weakest where a deep confined aquifer is decoupled from the surface. And integration does not dissolve ownership — the response to each outcome belongs to a different owner, which is why mechanism is taught here and institutional architecture routed. The verdict: one physical system, several policy systems, and answers fail when they confuse the two.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 5 for the weathered-mantle reservoir coupling with the crystalline-province premise; 5 for the erosion cascade evidenced from two independent sources plus the checklist corroboration; 4 for the conservation-to-recharge coupling with at least two named practices and their recorded data; 3 for the three conceded limits including the absence of quantification; 2 for the distinction between one physical system and several policy systems; 1 for a graded verdict. Deduct 4 for asserting any magnitude for the couplings, and 3 for presenting the four as independent subjects.

**Practice question 6 [20] — evidence and its abuse.** "In this subject, the most dangerous sentences are the ones with numbers in them." Critically examine, drawing on soil, landslide and groundwater data. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The proposition identifies a genuine hazard and overstates it; the distinction is between figures that do work and figures that do not. **The case for it.** One printed page gives, two paragraphs apart, both about twenty-one million hectares and about forty-five million hectares as the area subject to severe wind erosion; an institutional line quotes water-erosion loss as a number of million hectares **annually**, an area where a rate is required; a global degradation dataset alongside is of 1990 vintage and the Indian soil-loss figure beside it carries no attribution. In groundwater, a worked budget does not close, and two national percentages — 61 per cent for 2009 and 60.63 per cent for 2025 — look like a trend yet rest on different denominators and methods. **The case against it.** Other numbers are indispensable because each performs an argument. A clay fraction of sixty-two per cent or more explains regur's retention, cracking and narrow workable window. One cubic metre per hectare per week for thirty years equals fifteen centimetres off the whole surface, which makes invisible sheet erosion visible. Forty to eighty thousand cubic metres of debris per kilometre of hill road quantifies the cut, the spoil and the sediment at once. And the ravine concentration — 69.55 per cent of a national total in four states — is a structural claim no aggregate makes. The verdict: the danger lies in **undated, unattributed aggregates**, not in quantification, and the test is whether removing the figure would weaken the sentence.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 5 for at least three documented data failures, including one internal contradiction and one unit incoherence; 4 for the two incommensurable groundwater percentages explained; 4 for at least three load-bearing figures each tied to the argument it performs; 3 for the stated test distinguishing useful from decorative quantification; 2 for the dating discipline of agency, year, methodology and scale; 2 for a graded verdict rather than blanket scepticism. Deduct 4 for quoting any excluded aggregate as evidence, and 3 for concluding that all quantification should be avoided.

# REMEDIATION

Twenty-four errors that recur when this topic is used in an answer, each with the reason it fails, the repair, and the lesson that prevents it.

| # | ❌ Error | ⚠️ Why it fails | ❗ Repair | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | "Denudation means erosion" | Denudation contains four phases; erosion is one of them, and the omitted phase produces soil and the aquifer | "Denudation = weathering + erosion + transportation + deposition" | **L1** |
| R2 | Treating weathering only as preparation for erosion | Discards the two most answer-worthy products of the topic | "Regolith has three fates: stripped as sediment, organised as soil, or left in place as the reservoir" | **L1, L16** |
| R3 | "The slope was cold, so frost shattering was intense" | Permanent cold has no thaw phase and no liquid water | "Frost shattering needs repeated crossing of 0 °C **with water available** and a rock that admits it" | **L2** |
| R4 | ❌ "Carbonation turns limestone into liquid" | Melting is a phase change of the same substance; dissolution disperses different species in a solvent | "Carbonic acid converts carbonate to a soluble bicarbonate, **carried away in solution**" | **L3** |
| R5 | Listing three chemical weathering processes | One book's older, narrower scheme folds carbonation into solution | "Four: oxidation, carbonation, solution, hydrolysis" — name the three-fold scheme as older | **L3** |
| R6 | "Vegetation protects the rock beneath it" | Litter decay and root respiration load percolating water with CO₂ and organic acids | "A soil cover **enhances** chemical weathering; the active surface is the weathering front at the base" | **L4** |
| R7 | Reading a thin regolith as slow weathering | Thickness is production minus removal | "A scarp strips as fast as it weathers; a plateau retains" | **L4** |
| R8 | ❌ "Soil is crushed rock" | Regolith becomes soil only on horizon differentiation | "Last season's alluvium is fertile but **immature** — no horizons yet" | **L5** |
| R9 | "Rainforest soils are rich because the forest is luxuriant" | The nutrient capital is in the biomass and cycles rapidly | "Heavy leaching plus fast decay; which is why shifting cultivation needs long fallows" | **L5, L6** |
| R10 | "Regur is India's chernozem, black from humus" | Regur is poor in nitrogen and humus; its colour is mineral | "Black from basalt-inherited minerals; cotton suitability from retention plus self-mulching cracking" | **L6, L7** |
| R11 | ❌ "Laterite forms under perpetually wet conditions" | The dry phase is what hardens the crust | "Wet-and-dry alternation; cuts with a spade in the ground, **hardens irreversibly** on exposure" | **L3, L7** |
| R12 | Averaging the two desert-soil area figures | Different sources with different and unstated reporting bases | "Report both with both sources and state that the bases differ" | **L7** |
| R13 | Quoting a national soil-loss or degraded-area total | One printed page gives 21 and 45 million hectares for the same quantity | Use the **ravine state table** and the **69.55 per cent in four states** concentration ratio | **L8** |
| R14 | "Heavy annual rainfall causes the erosion" | Intensity, not total, drives detachment and runoff | "Not total rainfall but **how and when it comes**" | **L8** |
| R15 | Dismissing sheet erosion as minor | It removes the A horizon uniformly and invisibly | "1 m³/ha/week for 30 years = **15 cm off the entire surface**" | **L8** |
| R16 | Prescribing drainage and leaching for alkali land | Sodium disperses the clay and seals the soil; water will not move | "**Gypsum first** to displace sodium with calcium, then leach — and only with an outlet" | **L9** |
| R17 | Treating salinity as a natural arid-zone condition | Canal seepage creates both preconditions — a near-surface table and evaporative demand | "Waterlogging is itself a chief cause of salinity; it is a **human-induced** pathway" | **L6, L9** |
| R18 | ❌ "Rain lubricated the slope" | Lubrication is the least of water's three effects and conceals the greatest | "Pore pressure **subtracts from effective stress** and so from frictional strength" | **L10** |
| R19 | Treating deforestation as an erosion issue only | It removes the root contribution to cohesion, with a **delayed** minimum | "Cohesion falls as roots decay, so vulnerability peaks years after felling" | **L10** |
| R20 | ❌ "Soil creep is a small landslide" | Creep has no failure surface, no scar and no event | "Slow and imperceptible; diagnosed from **tilted trees, fences and posts**" | **L11** |
| R21 | Using "solifluction" for any wet flow | The term carries a climatic claim — that the ground freezes | "Strictly **periglacial**: saturated regolith over frozen ground. Use debris or mud flow" | **L11** |
| R22 | Naming rainfall as the cause of a landslide | A trigger explains when, not why the slope was able to fail | "Most contributions are **preparatory**; the same storm leaves most slopes standing" | **L12** |
| R23 | Reading a susceptibility map as a forecast or a risk map | It carries no time dimension and no exposure information | "Susceptibility is terrain propensity; hazard adds time; risk adds exposure and vulnerability" | **L12, L13** |
| R24 | Comparing 61 per cent (2009) with 60.63 per cent (2025) | Different agency-year, methodology, unit count and definitional base | "Quote both **with both dates** and state that they are not comparable" | **L17** |

## Map 2 — The six discriminations an examiner can check in one line each

```text
  1  WEATHERING   | in situ, nothing moves        || EROSION | an AGENT
                  |                               || moves it
                  | and MASS MOVEMENT: GRAVITY ALONE, no agent

  2  POROSITY     | how much is HELD              || PERMEABILITY | whether
                  | clay: ~half void, yields none || it MOVES | granite:
                  |                               || no pores, PERVIOUS
                  |                               || ALONG JOINTS

  3  WATER TABLE  | a REAL surface; top of the    || PIEZOMETRIC SURFACE |
                  | saturated zone; unconfined    || a PRESSURE statement;
                  |                               || imaginary; may lie
                  |                               || ABOVE THE GROUND

  4  SALINITY     | chlorides + sulphates of Na,  || ALKALINITY | sodium
                  | Ca, Mg; leach it out          || salts, esp. Na2CO3;
                  |                               || GYPSUM FIRST

  5  SUSCEPTIBILITY | propensity of the GROUND    || HAZARD | + a
                    | terrain only, no time       || probability in TIME
                    |                             ||
                    | VULNERABILITY | propensity  || RISK | hazard x
                    | of EXPOSED ELEMENTS         || exposure x vulnerability

  6  PREPARATORY  | years to decades; explains    || TRIGGERING | hours to
                  | WHERE; the object of          || days; explains WHEN;
                  | SUSCEPTIBILITY MAPPING        || the object of WARNING
```
