## Map 3 — The four asymmetries that carry the analytical marks

```text
  ASYMMETRY 1 -- OWNERSHIP
    5 demands OWNED (4 objective + 1 Mains)   vs   9 demands BRIDGED
    => the skill is RESTRAINT MADE VISIBLE: supply mechanism, name
       the owner, stop.  Nine scripts at L19.

  ASYMMETRY 2 -- THE RESOURCE AGAINST ITS USE
    Indus basin 79.29 %  vs  Brahmaputra 2.12 %   [1992-era, basin-wise]
    all-India 27.82 % -- ** BOTH EXTREMES INVISIBLE IN THE AVERAGE **
    and: the RICHEST province (Ganga-Brahmaputra alluvium) carries the
    MOST STRESSED tract (Punjab-Haryana-Rajasthan).
    => abundance PERMITTED the mismatch; it did not cause it, and it
       MASKED the over-draft for years.

  ASYMMETRY 3 -- HAZARD AGAINST ITS LETHALITY
    Fig. 8.18: most of India is recorded SAFE as far as landslides are
    concerned.  Yet Malpa (1998) and Malin (2014) destroyed ENTIRE
    VILLAGES -- neither recorded as exceptionally large.
    => casualties are "maximum in densely populated areas DOWN THE
       SLOPE": ** POSITION, not magnitude. **

  ASYMMETRY 4 -- DOCUMENTED DIRECTION AGAINST MISSING MAGNITUDE
    DOCUMENTED: the weathered mantle IS the reservoir; erosion LOWERS
      the table and RAISES landslide incidence; bunding INCREASED well
      recharge; afforestation INCREASES seepage.
    MISSING: not one of these carries a volume, a coefficient, a
      method or a measurement in any available source.
    => make the argument STRUCTURALLY.  Attaching a number to any of
       these couplings is INVENTING one.
```

# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

## A. Denudation, Regolith and the Four-Way Vocabulary

**Denudation = weathering + erosion + transportation + deposition.** ✅ **Regolith** = *"the mantle of weathered waste"*. ⚠️ Equilibrium statement: rock formed at depth is out of equilibrium at the surface; weathering is the **adjustment**. ❗ **One-line test:** nothing moved → weathering · an agent moved it → erosion · gravity alone → mass movement · all four phases → denudation. ⚠️ **Mass movement has no transporting agent**; water in it is a modifier of the gravitational problem. ❗ Regolith's **three fates**: stripped as sediment · organised as soil · left in place as the aquifer. ✅ **Owner's terminology control:** weathering ≠ erosion ≠ mass movement; porosity ≠ permeability; aquifer ≠ aquitard; water table ≠ piezometric surface; susceptibility ≠ hazard ≠ vulnerability ≠ risk. ✅ **Scale control:** grain/pore · soil profile · hillslope · catchment · aquifer · assessment unit are distinct. 🇮🇳 **Deccan basalt upland** — all four phases legible in one landscape; stratigraphy 🔁 routed. ⚠️ Residual: rainsplash and root prising straddle the weathering-erosion boundary.

## B. Mechanical Breakdown and Its Climatic Regimes

⚠️ **One mechanism, several sources of expansion; the rock fails in tension.** ✅ **Frost shattering** — water expands **about 9 per cent**; peaks in **humid micro-thermal, sub-arctic, polar and high-altitude** climates. ⚠️ Three preconditions: **water present · repeated crossing of 0 °C · joints or pores**. ❌ ✅ **Normalisation:** Leong gives *"one-tenth its volume"* and about **140 kg/cm² (2,000 lb/in²)**; ❗ the owner's **9 per cent** controls and the pressure figure is a dated order-of-magnitude anchor. ⚠️ **Insolation weathering** is **moisture-assisted**, not purely thermal. ✅ **Exfoliation** = "onion weathering"; debris = **scree**; ✅ **exfoliation domes** = concentric shells stripped. ⚠️ **Exfoliation ≠ pressure-release sheeting** — centimetre thermal shells against metre joints parallel to topography. ⚠️ **Block and granular disintegration are structurally selected**; **pressure release records erosion history**. ✅ **Salt crystallisation** in arid, coastal and irrigated settings. 🇮🇳 **Himalaya above the snow line** (angular scree → the material of L12) **against the Thar** (granular → the material of L8's wind). ❗ No Indian weathering rate asserted.

## C. The Four Chemical Reactions and the Limestone Correction

✅ **Oxidation · carbonation · solution · hydrolysis.** ⚠️ Master idea: minerals formed hot, dry and unoxidised are unstable at the surface; weathering is the **return journey**. ✅ **Oxidation** → rusty rotted rock; ⚠️ the oxide takes more volume, weakening the rock mechanically; 🇮🇳 red soils' colour from **diffused iron**, **90-100 % oxides** of Fe, Al, Ti, Mn. ✅ **Carbonation** — water + CO₂ → **weak carbonic acid** → carbonate **carried away in solution**; ⚠️ re-precipitated on evaporation as **kankar**. ✅ **Solution** — ions removed directly; ❗ runs **upward** by capillary rise in L9. ✅ **Hydrolysis** — **feldspar → clay minerals**; ⚠️ volumetrically dominant because silicates dominate the crust; ❗ **it manufactures the regolith and therefore the hard-rock aquifer**. ⚠️ **Rainwater is naturally mildly acidic** (atmospheric CO₂) and becomes **more acidic in the soil zone**. ❌ ✅ **Correction: limestone does not melt.** Melting = phase change of the same substance; dissolution = different species in a solvent; test by evaporation. ❌ ✅ **Leong's three-fold scheme is older and narrower.** ❌ ✅ **Hydration** is classed physical by one book, chemical by the owner. 🇮🇳 **Laterite** — Latin ***later*** = brick; **Buchanan, 1810, Malabar**; **Fermor**'s restriction; **Kuriyan** — *"end product of decomposition ... more than 200 cm"*; **2.48 lakh sq km**; ❗ needs a **wet-and-dry alternation**.

## D. The Six Controls and the Weathering Front

✅ **(i) rock structure and hardness (ii) climate (iii) topography and slope (iv) natural vegetation (v) time (vi) land use by man** — ✅ figure **after A.H. Strahler**; ✅ **climate decisive**; ✅ chemical weathering *"generally the more important"*. ⚠️ The structural variable is **joint density**, not scratch hardness. ⚠️ **Steep → stripped → thin; gentle → retained → deep.** ❗ Deep regolith is a **plateau and pediment** phenomenon. ⚠️ **Time**: depth is cumulative → a statement about **landscape age and stability**. ✅ **Land use is a named control**, which makes L8 and L12 continuous with the weathering block. ✅ ❗ **A soil cover ENHANCES chemical weathering** — organic acids and CO₂-enriched soil air; the active surface is the **weathering front at the base**. ⚠️ The profile is **self-deepening until drainage fails**. ✅ **Fig. 4.1 corestones** — joint-bounded blocks attacked from all faces, rounded outside-in; **wide spacing leaves a kernel**. ✅ **Tampin, Negri Sembilan** — **felspar weathers faster than quartz** → **coarse sandy residue**; deep tropical regoliths. ❗ **Cite Leong by local-scan page**; figure numbers only where visible. 🇮🇳 **Western Ghats laterite profile against the Aravalli crystallines** — same dials, opposite settings. ❗ **No regolith thickness asserted.**

## E. Pedogenesis, the Profile and the Soil System

✅ **Pedogenesis** = rock break-down + decayed organic material + living organisms + water + air. ✅ ❗ **Soil is not crushed rock** — it requires **horizon differentiation**; last season's alluvium is **fertile but immature**. ✅ **Four components**: mineral · organic · water · air; ⚠️ each failure mode is a later lesson. ✅ **Humus** = decomposed colloidal fraction; abundant under cool grassland, scarce in hot humid soils because **decay is fast**. ✅ **Rainforest soils are not rich** — capital in the **biomass**. ✅ **Horizons O · A · E · B · C · R**; ✅ **A = eluviation, B = illuviation**, **E** diagnostic of **podzolisation**. ✅ ❗ **The most testable pair: the B horizon is made of what A and E lost.** ⚠️ **Erosion takes the A horizon first.** ⚠️ Where **evaporation exceeds percolation the arrows reverse**. ✅ **Pedon** = smallest volume properly called a soil. ✅ **USDA texture triangle, 1975** (worked on a **Miami silt loam, Indiana**); ⚠️ Indian substitution — black soil at **≥ 62 % clay** plots deep in the clay field. ✅ **Structure = peds**; ⚠️ the fastest property to lose. ✅ 🇮🇳 **Soil → prosperity → density:** deep soils of the **Ganga plain and Kerala coast** against shallow coarse soils of **Telangana and Rajasthan**. ❗ **Soil sets probabilities, not destiny.** 🇮🇳 **Tarai** — a high water table truncates percolation → gleying and organic accumulation.

## F. Classification: Genetic, Morphometric and Indian

✅ **Zonal** (climate and vegetation, mature upland) · **intrazonal** (a local site factor overrides) · **azonal** (immature, ❗ **lacks a B horizon**). ⚠️ Indian application: **alluvial = azonal** (khadar newer and more fertile than bhangar, which carries kankar); **saline/alkaline = intrazonal**; **laterite and red = zonal**. ✅ **Six pedogenic directions:** podzolisation · **laterisation** · **calcification** (CaCO₃ → calcrete and **kankar**) · **salinisation/alkalisation** — ✅ *"a human-induced degradation pathway, not only a natural one"* · **gleying** (iron **reduced**) · humification/melanisation (**chernozem**). ✅ **Soil Taxonomy, 1975, US Soil Conservation Service, 11 orders**, world shares sourced to **Christopherson, *Geosystems*, 1995, p.464**; ⚠️ use the **design principle** — **diagnostic horizons and measurable properties** — not the recitation; ⚠️ it is **reproducible** and **agnostic about genesis**, which matters because soils are often **polygenetic**. ⚠️ Temperate schemes strain in the tropics on **depth · irreversible hardening · seasonal alternation**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Regur as "tropical chernozem"** — ✅ third largest group, **~50 m ha = 15 %** of reporting area, parent material **the weathered rocks of Cretaceous lava**, **50-75 cm** rainfall belt, cracks **10-15 cm**. ❗ **The analogy fails on causation:** chernozem is black from **humus**; regur from **basalt-inherited minerals**, and is **poor in nitrogen and organic matter**. ❗ **No crosswalk exists between the eight Indian groups and Soil Taxonomy's orders.**

## G. India's Parent Materials and Eight Soil Groups

✅ **Six parent-material classes:** (i) ancient crystalline and metamorphic (ii) **Cuddapah and Vindhyan** (iii) **Gondwana** (iv) **Deccan basalts** (v) Tertiary and Mesozoic sedimentaries of extra-peninsular India (vi) recent and sub-recent. ❗ **The same six reappear as the eight groundwater provinces.** ✅ **Alluvial** — most extensive; **khadar > bhangar**; exposed to waterlogging and salinity under canal irrigation. ✅ **Regur** — clay **≥ 62 %**, **10 %** iron oxide, **6-8 %** alumina, **9-10 %** lime and Mg carbonates, **potash < 0.5 %**, kankar nodules. ❗ **Basaltic lava, not black minerals and not alluvium**; cotton suitability = **retention + self-mulching cracking**. ✅ **Red and yellow** — **3.5 lakh sq km = 10.6 %**; **diffused iron**; **90-100 % oxides**. ✅ **Laterite** — **2.48 lakh sq km**; cuts with a spade in the ground, **hardens irreversibly**; low base-exchange; tea, coffee, rubber, cinchona, coconut, arecanut, paddy in low ground. ✅ **Arid/desert** — **1.42 lakh sq km = 4.32 %**, sand **90-95 %**, clay **5-10 %**, **Indira Gandhi Canal Command Area**; ❗ **a second source gives ~15 million ha = 4.42 %; both reported, neither averaged.** ✅ **Saline and alkaline — 68,000 sq km**; names **reh, kallar, usar, thur, rakar, karl, chopan**; **UP ~1.25 m ha, Punjab ~1.21 m ha** under *usar*; ✅ **Gulf of Khambhat** salt-laden tides; **Narmada, Tapi, Mahi, Sabarmati** estuaries infertile. ✅ **Peaty and marshy — 10-40 % organic**; ***kari*** in **Kottayam and Alappuzha**; also coastal Odisha, Tamil Nadu, Sunderbans, Bihar, Almora. ✅ **Forest and mountain — 2.85 lakh sq km = 8.67 %**; **highly erosion-prone once cleared**. ✅ **Eight problems:** erosion · fertility deficiency · desertification · waterlogging · salinity and alkalinity · wasteland · **over-exploitation from population growth** · **urban and transport encroachment**. ❌ ✅ **Superseded:** the Soil Survey Division's **seven-type list, Table 10.17, Chatterji 1973 and 1986**; ❗ **its Mountain and Desert rows are garbled and are not quoted.** 🇮🇳 **Rajmahal Hills = the eastern limit of regur.**

## H. India's Erosion Processes and Eroded Landscapes

✅ **Definition:** removal *"more rapidly than the various soil forming processes can replace it"* — ⚠️ **comparative, and about the A horizon**. ✅ **Ladder:** splash → sheet → rill → gully → **ravine (30 m or more)** → **badland (immediate drop of 3-4 m)**. ✅ **Splash:** drops at **~10 m/sec**; **sands and silts detach most readily**; impact **seals the surface**. ✅ ❗ **1 m³/ha/week for 30 years = 15 cm off the entire surface.** ✅ **Not total rainfall but how and when it comes.** ✅ **Scaling law** (stated "theoretically"): double the flow → carry **×4**, scour **×32**, particle size **×64**; ⚠️ **slope length matters as much as angle**. ✅ **Wind:** **suspension** (humus, clay, silt — the fertility fraction, **exported**) · **saltation** · **surface creep**; ✅ **desert pavement** = armour of fragments **> 1.00 mm**; **1.0-2.0 mm** and above cannot be rolled. ✅ **Sea and glacial erosion** named as further agents. ✅ **Fig. 7.2 legend:** gully + sheet · sheet · desert (aeolian) · shifting cultivation · glacial · coastal · ❗ **limit of wind erosion (a line)**. ✅ **Ravines: MHA working group, 1971 — 39.75 lakh ha / 18 states**, with **27.65 lakh ha = 69.55 %** in UP, MP, Rajasthan, Gujarat; **Chambal Development Scheme survey ~50,600 ha at 4.5-6.0 m**; **MP 4-8 lakh ha**, of which **2.4 lakh ha** in **Gwalior, Morena, Bhind**; **UP:** Chambal, Yamuna, Gomati, Son — **Agra, Etawah, Jalaun**. ✅ **Table 7.1 (lakh ha):** UP 12.30 · MP 6.83 · Rajasthan 4.52 · Gujarat 4.00 · Punjab 1.20 · WB 1.04 · Bihar 0.60 · TN 0.60 · Maharashtra 0.20 — ❗ **no source line visible on the page**. ✅ **Chambal-Yamuna badlands ~32 lakh ha, 1,000 years at ~0.25 tonnes daily.** ✅ **Fig. 6.6** separates **ravines and bluff** from **cultivated land**; gully erosion also in the **Shiwalik tracts** and the **Ghats**. ✅ **Human factors:** ***chos*** — **~100 streams across 130 km, worst in Hoshiarpur**; overgrazing; **shifting cultivation >15 lakh ha cleared yearly, 45 lakh ha affected** (Assam 207,287 ha · Tripura 41,963 ha · Manipur 21,862 ha · Odisha 33,08,502 ha); **Nilgiri potato on slopes over 60 %, yields down ~50 %**; ✅ **land steeper than 1 in 4 should not be cultivated**. ✅ **Ganga ~30 m tonnes/yr; Brahmaputra ~10 m tonnes.** ✅ **Kerala: ~32 km of a 560 km coast under severe sea erosion.** ❌ ❗ **EXCLUDED: the 21-against-45 million hectare contradiction and its travelling aggregates; the ICAR unit error; 1990 GLASOD global figures and the un-attributed Indian tonnage; undated desert tonnages.**

## I. Salinity, Waterlogging and the Reclamation Decision

✅ **Salinity** = white crust **raised by capillary action**; **chlorides and sulphates of Na, Ca, Mg**. ✅ **Alkalinity** = **sodium salts, especially sodium carbonate**. ✅ **Sandy → alkalinity; loamy → salinity-alkalinity.** ❗ **Alkali needs gypsum first**; leaching alone fails because sodium disperses the clay. ✅ **About 80 lakh ha = 2.43 % of India**, worst in **western UP**. ✅ **Waterlogging ~12 million ha — half coastal, half inland in command areas (Fig. 7.5)**; causes: flat surfaces, **saucer-like depressions**, **seepage from unlined canals**. ✅ ❗ **Waterlogging is itself a chief cause of salinity.** ✅ **(a) Surface drainage** — open, with an **adequate outlet**; for deep soils, low infiltration, high rainfall intensity, level terrain, high table. ✅ **(b) Vertical drainage** — any bore or well extracting the water; needs **a favourable aquifer** and **re-usable quality**. ✅ ❗ **Tubewell pumping lowered the Punjab-Haryana water table and reclaimed waterlogged saline land.** ✅ **Alkali chemistry:** **gypsum to the upper 15 cm** · sulphuric acid, **sulphur, pyrite** · **rice husk and straw** · flooding — ❗ **caveat: it can accumulate saline water downstream.** ✅ **Salt-tolerant crops: rice, berseem, barley, jowar, sugarcane.** ✅ **Doctrine:** **S.P. Chatterjee** — *"the greatest single evil to Indian agriculture and animal husbandry"*; **G.T. Renner** — *"the greatest good to the largest number for the longest time"*; **S.I. Kayastha**; ✅ **about two-thirds of arable land needs conservation**. ✅ **Forest norm (dated): 20-25 % healthy, raised to 33 % in the Second Five Year Plan — 20 % plains, 60 % hills.** ✅ **Practices:** rotation · **strip cropping (7.6)** · early-maturing varieties · **contour ploughing (7.7)** · **terracing and contour bunding (7.8)** · checking shifting cultivation · **ploughing perpendicular to the wind**. ✅ ❗ **Thresholds: bunding less effective above 8 % (1 in 12); nothing terraced above 20 % (1 in 5); fields steeper than 15 % (1 in 6) leave the plough.** ✅ **Institutional record to 2003-04:** First Plan adoption · Department of Agriculture and Cooperation division · Third Plan siltation scheme · **Flood-Prone Rivers from the Sixth Plan** · **Ninth Plan clubbing — 53 catchments, 27 states, 96.14 m ha total, 26 m ha priority, 5.86 m ha treated**; ***usar*** — Seventh Plan (Haryana, Punjab, UP) → Eighth Plan (Gujarat, MP, Rajasthan), **0.62 of 3.5 m ha reclaimed**; **ravine reclamation 1987-88, to the state sector 1991-92 by the National Development Council, ~1.266 lakh ha**; **shifting cultivation 1986-87 → 1987-88 nine states, 26,532 families by 1990-91, 67,000 ha in the Eighth Plan, 1.5 lakh ha to March 2002, 0.4 lakh ha in the Tenth Plan**; **All India Soil and Land Survey Organisation uses remote sensing**. ❗ **The answer-worthy figure is the completion ratio.** ✅ ❗ **COUPLING: contour bunding gave 25 % yield gains and *"also increased the water recharging capacity of wells in the adjoining areas"*;** ✅ **National Commission on Agriculture — 35 % from bunding, 60 % from levelling** (semi-arid alluvial UP); **15-65 %** potato gains from **bench terracing, Kunda catchment, Nilgiris**; ✅ **afforestation increases seepage to the water table**; ✅ **shelterbelts** in Gujarat, Haryana, Rajasthan, western MP.

## J. Slope Mechanics: The Force Balance and Pore-Water Pressure

⚠️ **Driving = W sin b**; **resisting = c + (W cos b − u) tan φ**; **FoS = resisting / driving**. ❗ **u subtracts inside the resisting term** → a slope fails **with nothing added**. ⚠️ **Water does three things:** **adds mass** (modest net effect — it raises both sides) · **raises pore pressure** → effective stress down → friction down (❗ **dominant**) · **destroys apparent cohesion** in sand and **softens clay**. ❌ ❗ **"Lubrication" is the weakest effect and conceals the strongest.** ⚠️ **Cohesion includes root reinforcement** — which is why ✅ increasing vegetation cover is *"the cheapest and most effective way of arresting landslides"*, binding the top layer to the layers below. ✅ **Slump mechanism:** **permeable debris on impermeable clay → perched water body → curved slip surface → rotational failure**; diagnostic — the block **back-tilts**. ⚠️ **Antecedent moisture** explains the late-monsoon clustering. ⚠️ **Toe cutting removes a buttress** — a small excavation lowers the FoS of a large mass. ⚠️ **Deforestation cuts c with a delayed minimum** as roots decay. ⚠️ **Rapid drawdown** removes external support before pore pressure dissipates. ⚠️ **Steepening hurts twice** — raises sin b, lowers cos b. 🇮🇳 **Western Ghats scarp** — every term adverse at once. ❗ **No measured c, φ, FoS or regolith thickness for any Indian slope.** ⚠️ Residual: limit equilibrium is **explanatory, not predictive**; real failure is progressive.

## K. The Mass-Movement Family and the Solifluction Correction

⚠️ **Two axes: velocity and water content**; the **internal-deformation test** — arrives recognisable → **slide**; arrives mixed → **flow**. ✅ **Leong's three families:** soil creep · soil flow · landslides (slumping and sliding); ⚠️ **fall and topple added here, with the addition declared**. ✅ **Khullar:** a landslide is rapid downslope movement of rock, soil and vegetation under gravity; natural removal from slopes = **mass wasting**. ✅ **Creep** — slow, near-continuous; ✅ **tilts trees, fences and posts**; debris at the slope foot; ❌ ❗ **not rapid and not visible**; ⚠️ mechanism — expansion and contraction lift particles normal to the slope and let them settle vertically. ❌ ✅ ❗ **SOLIFLUCTION is strictly PERIGLACIAL** — saturated regolith over **frozen ground**, where the frozen substrate is the mechanism; both books use it loosely; ❗ in India it is a **high-Himalayan** phenomenon, and warm-wet cases are **debris flow** or **mud flow**. ✅ **Irish bog-bursts** retained as a one-sentence end-member. ✅ **Subsidence** (advanced owner) — weathered, water-saturated debris **over-loaded by unplanned construction**; a **slow mass-movement hazard**; ⚠️ **predominantly vertical**; two mechanisms — **loading** and **abstraction** (pore pressure falls, the skeleton carries more load). 🔁 ❗ **Liquefaction is named in the same table and is owned by the vulcanism-and-earthquakes topic.** 🔁 ✅ **Snow is a medium of mass movement** — one contrast sentence; **avalanche doctrine routed in full**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Kanara coast cliff fall** — undercutting → overhang → tensile failure → detachment; ❗ **mass movement with no water inside the material**.

## L. Landslide Causation: Four Classes and the Attribution Rule

✅ ❗ **Owner's control:** *"intense rain is a trigger, not a complete landslide cause"*; test **antecedent moisture, discontinuities, toe cutting, drainage, recharge and demand**. ✅ **Source:** ***Manual on Natural Disaster Management in India* (2001), National Centre for Disaster Management, p.68.** ✅ **[1] Ground:** weak, sensitive or **weathered** materials · adverse structure (**joints, fissures**) · variation in **permeability and plasticity**. ✅ **[2] Morphological:** tectonic or volcanic **uplift** · **wind or water erosion** · **scour** · **deposition loading the crest** · vegetation removal by **fire or drought**. ✅ **[3] Physical:** **prolonged precipitation** · **rapid draw-down** · earthquake · volcanic eruption · thawing · shrink and swell · **artesian pressure**. ✅ **[4] Man-made:** **excavation, especially at the slope toe** · crest loading · reservoir draw-down · **deforestation** · irrigation · mining · vibration · **water impoundment and leakage from utilities**. ❗ ⚠️ **Preparatory → WHERE (years to decades); triggering → WHEN (hours to days).** ✅ ❗ **Erosion is formally a listed landslide cause** — independent confirmation of the cascade. ✅ **Hill-road anchor: 40,000-80,000 cubic metres per kilometre**, which slides downslope, **kills vegetation and chokes mountain streams**; ⚠️ it quantifies **cut, spoil and sediment** at once. ✅ **Elements at risk (Fig. 8.17):** settlements **on steep slopes · at the slope toe · at the mouths of streams emerging from mountain valleys**; buildings **without appropriate foundations**; roads, lines and **buried utilities**; onset **gradual but frequently without warning**; risk from **geology, hydrology, vegetation cover, past occurrence and consequence**. ✅ ❗ **Susceptibility · hazard · vulnerability · risk** kept apart. 🇮🇳 📰 **Joshimath** — ✅ *"no single trigger explains the whole crisis"*; 📰 reported findings: **old landslide and morainic debris foundation · internal erosion from seepage · blocked and diverted drainage · construction beyond bearing capacity · wastewater discharge · seismically active setting**; ❗ five of six **preparatory**, four of those **man-made and decades old**; ❗ **classed as subsidence**; ❗ **no figure quoted; report bodies not retrievable.** ⚠️ Residual: the checklist **does not weight its items**.

## M. India's Landslide Geography and Physical Mitigation

✅ **VERY HIGH:** young **Himalaya** · **Andaman and Nicobar** · steep rainy slopes of the **Western Ghats and Nilgiris** · the **north-east**. ✅ **HIGH:** same conditions, lower intensity and frequency — all Himalayan states and the north-east **except the Assam plains**. ✅ **MODERATE TO LOW:** **Ladakh and Spiti** · **Aravalli** · **rain-shadow Ghats** · **Deccan plateau** — ❗ failures there arise chiefly from **mining and subsidence** across eleven named states. ✅ ❗ **OTHER AREAS "safe as far as landslides are concerned":** Rajasthan, Haryana, UP, Bihar, **WB except Darjeeling**, **Assam except Karbi Anglong**, southern coastal regions. ⚠️ **Most of India is not landslide country** — the hazard is **concentrated**, which is what makes zonation worthwhile. ✅ **Consequences:** obstruct river flow → **flash floods**; floods in the **upper Ganga and Brahmaputra**; road and rail blockage. ✅ **Zonation mapping in use since the 1980s (Fig. 8.18)**; ✅ casualties **maximum in densely populated areas down the slope**. 📰 **GSI, Ministry of Mines — National Landslide Susceptibility Mapping at 1:50,000**, reported over ~**4.3 lakh sq km / 19 states and UTs**; inventory **>91,000**, ~**33,904** field-validated; **1:10,000 and 1:5,000 for 200 critical sectors**, ~**160 completed as reported in 2025**; **Bhukosh · Bhusanket · Bhooskhalan**; **Regional Landslide Forecasting System** and an experimental **Landslide Early Warning System**. 📰 **NRSC/ISRO *Landslide Atlas of India*** — reported ~**80,000 landslides, 1998-2022**, ranking **147 districts / 17 states and 2 UTs** by **exposure**. ❗ **Susceptibility maps preparatory factors; forecasting adds the trigger; risk adds exposure.** 🔁 ❗ **The National Landslide Risk Management Strategy (2019) and the National Landslide Risk Mitigation Programme are named and routed.** ✅ **Himalayan causation:** **Seismic Zone V**, fragile ecology, **unplanned development, indiscriminate felling, urbanisation**; local effects — terraced fields destroyed **beyond renovation**, roads closed, ❗ **water courses choked so villagers upslope lose irrigation water** → **the damage footprint is not the area covered**. ✅ **Table 8.10:** **Kalighat 1993** · **Nilgiris 1993 (40 dead, over 600 families shifted)** · **Malpa, 12-18 Aug 1998 (entire village washed away; about 384 killed, including 60 Mansarovar pilgrims)** · **Uttarkashi, 24 Sept-10 Oct 2003 (Varunavat Parvat; 362 families; ~3,000 evacuated; Gangotri highway)** · **Badrinath, 6 July 2004** · **Uttarakhand, 16 June 2013** · **Malin, 80 km north of Pune, 30 July 2014 (whole village buried; more than a hundred feared dead)**. ✅ **Mitigation:** **hazard mapping · land-use control · retaining walls, as on the Konkan Railway · surface drainage control · engineered foundations and flexible buried utilities · ❗ increasing vegetation cover, the cheapest and most effective**. ⚠️ **Each maps to a balance term.** 🇮🇳 **Malpa and Malin — lethality by position, not magnitude**; ❗ no cause asserted for either.

## N. Groundwater Occurrence: The Property Pair and the Two Surfaces

⚠️ **Porosity = how much is held; permeability = whether it moves** — decided by **size and connection** of voids. ✅ **Most porous rocks are also permeable**; the examinable content is the **off-diagonals**. ✅ **Clay** — high porosity, **impermeable**; ⚠️ its porosity is nearly all **specific retention**. ✅ **Granite** — crystalline, effectively non-porous, yet **"pervious along joints"**; ⚠️ **primary against secondary porosity** is the hinge of the province argument. ⚠️ **Aquifer · aquitard · aquiclude · aquifuge** defined by **behaviour**. ✅ **Zone of aeration** (soil-water belt, intermediate belt, **capillary fringe**) above; **zone of saturation** below. ✅ **Water table** = the **real** top of saturation in an **unconfined** aquifer; ❗ **piezometric surface** = the level to which water **would rise** in a **confined** aquifer — **imaginary**, above the aquifer top, possibly **above the ground**. ⚠️ **Specific yield + specific retention = porosity.** ✅ Groundwater occupies **pores, cavities and cracks** and is **everywhere beneath the surface, including beneath deserts**; ✅ **infiltration equals or exceeds surface runoff in many areas**. ❗ ⚠️ **Presence is not renewability** — desert groundwater may be a **stock emplaced under a past climate**. 🔁 ✅ One framing line only: water is **renewable yet finite**; **about 3 %** of the world's water is fresh, **roughly one-third of that inaccessible**. 🇮🇳 **Deccan trap — vesicular yet deficient**, because **vesicles are unconnected**. ⚠️ Residual: permeability is **scale-dependent and directional**.

## O. Springs, Artesian Geometry and the Four-In Four-Out Budget

✅ **A spring is the saturated zone intersecting the surface**; six geometries — **tilted strata · joint · dyke · scarp-foot · dip-slope · Vauclusian**; 🔁 the karst assemblage routed. ⚠️ **A dyke spring is the natural analogue of the engineered subsurface dyke.** ⚠️ **Perched water:** an impermeable lens supports a saturated zone **above the regional table** → hillside springs, and ❗ a slope saturated at a contact while the valley table is far lower. ✅ **Artesian conditions, all four required:** **downfolded syncline · permeable aquifer confined between impermeable clays · recharge at the exposed edges · sufficient hydraulic head**; ✅ cases — **Great Artesian Basin, Australia** (Queensland sheep farming) and the **Great Plains, USA**. ❌ ❗ **"All wells are artesian" is false** — a **geometry, not a depth**; **flowing** artesian = the piezometric surface lies above the ground there. ✅ ❗ **Artesian water may be hot or saline and therefore unsuitable for irrigation.** ✅ **Budget — supply:** (i) **precipitation infiltration** (ii) **natural recharge from streams, lakes and ponds** (iii) **groundwater inflow** (iv) **recharge from irrigation, reservoirs and artificial-recharge schemes**. ✅ **Budget — disposal:** (i) **evaporation from the capillary fringe where the table is shallow + transpiration** (ii) **natural discharge by seepage and spring flow** (iii) **groundwater outflow** (iv) **artificial discharge by pumping or flowing wells and drains**. ❗ ⚠️ **"Over-exploitation" = disposal exceeds supply, FOR A STATED UNIT AND PERIOD.** ❗ **Items (iii) in both columns:** an aquifer's boundaries are **hydrological, not administrative**. ⚠️ Disposal item (i) is **also the salinisation engine**. ❗ ❌ **EXCLUDED: the source's worked arithmetic does not close** — not reproduced, not reconstructed; ⚠️ teach the **units problem** (m ha m against BCM). ✅ **Leong:** *"All wells bored by Man tend to deplete groundwater resources"* — ⚠️ a **tendency**, ❗ too strong unqualified. 🇮🇳 **Bhabar-tarai spring line** — streams **vanish into the bhabar** and **seep out in the tarai**; ⚠️ a **permeability boundary made visible**. ⚠️ Residual: the budget is a **steady-state identity** without storage change or lag.

## P. India's Eight Groundwater Provinces and the Integrating Sentence

✅ ❗ ***"the near surface, weathered mantle forms the all important ground water reservoir, and the source for circulation of ground water through underlying fracture systems."*** ⚠️ **The mantle stores; the fractures transmit.** ✅ **Deep weathered pediments, low-lying valleys and abandoned river channels** hold porous material; ✅ **saturated systems generally to about 100 m**; ✅ **friable semi-consolidated sandstones** give moderate yields with **auto-flowing zones not uncommon**. ✅ **P1 Pre-Cambrian Crystalline — DEFICIENT**, over **half the country's area** (TN, AP, Telangana, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Dandakaranya, Bundelkhand, **Aravalli**). ✅ **P2 Pre-Cambrian Sedimentary — not much suitable** (**Cuddapah and Vindhyan**). ✅ **P3 Gondwana — GOOD AQUIFERS** (**Barakar and Godavari**). ✅ **P4 Deccan Trap — DEFICIENT**, **~1,200 m of impermeable basalt**; water only in **fractures · weathered *moorum* · intertrappean beds · vesicles and amygdales**. ✅ **P5 Cenozoic Sedimentary — GOOD AQUIFERS** (AP, TN, Kerala, Gujarat coasts; **tertiary sandstones**). ✅ **P6 Cenozoic Fault Basin — GOOD RESOURCE** (**Narmada, Purna, Tapi rift; 80-160 m** of sand, silt and clay). ✅ **P7 Ganga-Brahmaputra Alluvial — THE RICHEST PROVINCE** (**bhabar, tarai and axial belts**; high water table). ✅ **P8 Himalayan — little significance; springs common, wells rare.** ✅ **Four controls:** **climate · relief features · geological structure · the hydrological setup**; ⚠️ **three of four are also weathering controls**, because the reservoir **is** the weathered mantle. ✅ **Coastal and deltaic tracts:** thick alluvium, **highly productive aquifers**, ✅ but **salinity hazards impose quality constraints**. ✅ **Saline wedge:** **over-pumping or lower recharge → freshwater head falls → the interface moves landward and upward → wells and soils become saline**; ⚠️ small head loss, large interface movement; **slow to reverse**. 🔁 ❗ **Coastal-intrusion response is owned by the environment owner.** 🇮🇳 ***Moorum*** is **a weathering product functioning as an aquifer**; **intertrappean beds** are a **confined geometry**. ❗ **Date the scheme: R.L. Singh, 1971**; **lithology has not changed, extraction has**; ❗ do not convert a verbal verdict into a yield.

## Q. Extraction Stress, the Dating Method and Quality

✅ **Lineage:** **Khosla 1949 — 167.4 m ha m** run-off · **National Commission on Agriculture 1976 — 67 m ha m total, 35 usable, 26 for irrigation** · **1979 — the Ground Water Over Exploitation Committee under the Agriculture Refinance and Development Corporation** · **Ground Water Estimation Committee 1984 norms** · **Planning Commission — about 178 m ha m**. ❗ Not mutually comparable; ❗ units are **m ha m**, not BCM. ✅ **Table 15.4 (source given as *India 1992: A Reference Annual*, p.419) — level of development:** **Indus 79.29 %** · Madras composite + south TN 45.94 % · Cauvery 44.72 % · Ganga 30.79 % · **all-India 27.82 %** · Brahmani-Baitarni 5.16 % · Mahanadi 4.32 % · Meghna 3.21 % · **Brahmaputra 2.12 %**. ❗ ⚠️ **A 37-fold range with both extremes invisible in the average.** ✅ **The book records a shift to state and UT reporting**, with **2009**: replenishable **> 433 BCM/yr**, irrigation **221.42**, other uses **21.89**, **level of development 61 %**; ❗ the procedural claim is **book-era and unconfirmed**. 📰 ✅ **CGWB, Ministry of Jal Shakti, *National Compilation on Dynamic Ground Water Resources of India, 2025*:** recharge **448.52 BCM** · extractable **407.75 BCM** · extraction **247.22 BCM** · **stage of extraction 60.63 %**; of **6,762 assessment units** — **Safe 73.14 % · Semi-critical 11.21 % · Critical 2.97 % · Over-exploited 10.80 % (730 units) · Saline 1.88 %**; over-exploited share reported down from **17.2 % (2017)** to **10.8 % (2025)**, concentrated **north-west, west and south**. ❗ **61 % (2009) and 60.63 % (2025) are not the same statistic — different agency-year, methodology, unit count and definitional base. Do not subtract.** ✅ **The argument:** Haryana, Punjab and Rajasthan get **under 40 cm**, are **deficient in surface water**, and **exploit more than 100 per cent** of available groundwater via **tube wells**; demand rose with the **Green Revolution in the 1960s**; ✅ **the region is climatically more suited to wheat but farmers' preference for rice changed the entire scenario**; ✅ **"granary of today will be barren land tomorrow"** — ❗ **a conditional**. ✅ **UP's irrigation resource 46.00 BCM/yr**; **UP and Bihar over 72 %**, **Gujarat over 75 %**; replenishable from **75.25 BCM/yr (UP)** to **0.044 BCM/yr (Mizoram)**. ✅ ❗ **The counterpoint is mandatory** — groundwater irrigation created the region's **productivity and drought resilience**; the failure is of **governance, energy pricing and crop choice**. ✅ **Quality:** governed by **waste disposal and land use**; ✅ **disposal through wells and sinkholes injects contaminants directly**; ✅ pollution may persist **decades or even centuries**; ✅ **most drought-prone areas depend largely on groundwater**. ❌ ✅ **Owner's trap: not only quantity — quality too.**

## R. Artificial Recharge: Designs, Derivations and the Conditions of Effectiveness

✅ **Harvesting** = capture **before it is lost as surface run-off**; ✅ **artificial recharge** = augmentation **at a rate exceeding that under natural conditions** — ❗ which embeds a measurement obligation. ❗ It is **supply item (iv) of the budget**. ✅ **Need, seven items**, of which ❗ **infiltration has decreased drastically in urban areas because open ground has been paved**. ✅ **Advantages, eleven items**, of which ❗ ***"the aquifer itself serves as the distribution system"***, **no land wasted and no population displaced**, **flood hazards reduced** and **soil erosion reduced**. ✅ **Four potential-area criteria:** declining levels · **substantially de-saturated aquifers** · lean-month inadequacy · **urbanisation-driven infiltration loss**. ✅ **Urban:** pit · trench · tubewell · recharge well. ✅ **Rural:** **gully plug · contour bund · gabion · percolation tank · check dam/cement plug/nala bund · recharge shaft · dugwell recharge · ground water dams and subsurface dykes** — ❗ **this is the conservation list renamed**. ✅ **Fig. 15.7 pit:** roof **~100 sq m**, alluvial with permeable rock at or near surface; **1-2 m wide, 2-3 m deep**; **boulders 5-20 cm / gravel 5-10 mm / coarse sand 1.5-2 mm**, ✅ **so silt deposits on the sand and can be removed**; roof mesh; desilting chamber. ✅ **Fig. 15.8 trench:** roof **200-300 sq m**; **0.5-1 m × 1-1.5 m × 10-20 m**; **15 % coarse sand, 20 % 20 mm gravel, 50 % 40 mm boulder**. ✅ **Fig. 15.9 tubewell recharge:** where **shallow aquifers have dried up**; **10 cm PVC roof drains**; **first-flush diversion through the bottom of the drain pipe**; inline filter **1-1.2 m**, **15 cm** for roofs under **150 sq m**, **20 cm** above; **6.25 cm reducer each end**; **three chambers** so filter materials do not mix. ❗ **All three carry a silt trap, a graded filter and a maintenance path** — because injection **bypasses the soil filter** and contamination persists for decades. ✅ **Table 15.10**, source **CGWB, *Rainwater Harvesting Techniques to Augment Ground Water*, 2003**: rainfall **20-3,000 mm** against roofs **100-2,000 sq m**. ⚠️ ❗ **DERIVED: 100 mm over 100 sq m = 10 cu m gross, tabulated as 8 → a runoff coefficient of about 0.8. Tagged as an inference; the table states none.** ✅ **Demand side: about 12.5 litres per flush against cisterns needing 5-7**; greywater reuse; ✅ **World Water Day, 22 March**, from a **1992 United Nations resolution**. ✅ **Bounded comparator only:** attributed to **Jayanta Bandhopadhyay, IIM Kolkata**, citing CGWB — **37 BCM recharged locally at about Rs 24,500 crore** against **Rs 5,60,000 crore for 175 BCM from interlinking**; 🔁 **interlinking itself is owned elsewhere**. ❗ **NOT QUANTIFIED:** the **2 per cent of India's land area** harvesting claim — **no period is stated on the page**. 📰 🇮🇳 **Atal Bhujal Yojana, Ministry of Jal Shakti, with World Bank support** — reported **Rs 6,000 crore**, half loan and half central; **seven states**; **8,203 gram panchayats, 229 blocks, 80 districts**; **community Water Budgets and Water Security Plans**; **Program-for-Results**; improvement reported in **180 of 229 blocks**; 🔁 **governance design routed**. ❗ **"Effective" = aquifer-appropriate · sized to catchment · maintained · protected · monitored · matched to demand.**

## S. Answer Craft: Spines, Routes and the Consolidated Discipline

✅ **Spine 1 (15 marks, groundwater and food security), seven steps:** thesis · physical system · imbalance · transmission · ❗ **balance** · response logic (demand / supply / institutional) · graded conclusion — ❗ **a declared bounded bridge**. ✅ **Spine 2 (10 marks, soil as hinge):** parent material and climate → pedogenic direction → property → crop and settlement → degradation risk → conservation response; ❗ **soil sets probabilities, not destiny**. ⚠️ **Spine 3 (slope failure):** setting → force balance → preparatory classes → trigger → why the trigger is not the cause → India geography → physical mitigation → ❗ **route**. ⚠️ **Spine 4 (15 marks, urban harvesting):** reframe as **restoration of a destroyed recharge pathway** → definitions → the four criteria → one design → ❗ **the five conditions** → the harvest table with the inferred coefficient → **complements, not alternatives**. ✅ **Directive decoding** — nine rows, of which ⚠️ **"how can it be made effective" asks for conditions of success, not an inventory**. ✅ **Four evidence units:** parent rock against climate · luxuriance is not fertility · irrigation degrades what it makes productive · ⚠️ **the integrating cascade**. 🔁 ❗ **Nine routing scripts** — 2024 GS-I → food security · 2025 GS-III intrusion → environment · 2025 GS-III depletion → economy · 2019 and 2021 GS-III and 2021 GS-I landslides → disaster management · 2020 GS-I desertification → environment · 2026 GS-I aeolian → arid processes · 2026 GS-III infrastructure → disaster management. ❗ **The removal test:** delete the institution's name — if a physical statement survives, it is this topic's. ❗ **Ten map items:** Chambal-Yamuna ravine tract · Shiwalik *chos* · **the limit of wind erosion (a line)** · the north-east shifting-cultivation belt · the eight soil groups · the Very High landslide zone · the eight groundwater provinces · the bhabar-tarai spring line · the Punjab-Haryana-Rajasthan over-extraction belt · the coastal saline-intrusion tracts. ❗ **Forty-seven consolidated traps**, of which the costliest are **trespass** and **undated or contradicted statistics**. ❗ **Six things not to write:** no national soil-loss figure · no answer letter · no Indian weathering rate, regolith thickness or stability value · no asserted cause for an individual landslide and no Joshimath figure · no institutional architecture · no recharge magnitude.

## Scope, boundary and typing

- A **Mains-focused** edition of Geography Topic 04, addressing **General Studies Paper I directly** under the geophysical-phenomena clause, and the General Studies Paper III disaster clause **as support only**. It preserves the **integrated topic — the world-process half and the India-applied half in one file** — across **nineteen lessons**, structured as **weathering (L1-L4), soil and India erosion and degradation (L5-L9), mass movement and landslides (L10-L13), groundwater (L14-L18) and answer craft (L19)**.
- **The integration is structural, not stylistic.** ❗ Every world-process lesson — L1 to L6, L10, L11, L14 and L15 — **closes on a named India hook**, and the India-applied lessons L7 to L9, L12, L13 and L16 to L18 carry the applied argument. ❗ **The erosion-to-water-table-to-landslide cascade is introduced at L8 and named again at L9, L12, L17 and L19**, and the conservation-to-recharge coupling closes the loop at L9 and L18.
- **Ownership stated precisely.** ✅ The controlling clause is binding: *"Topic 05 owns fluvial erosion, drainage and river-basin transfers; Topic 07 desertification; Topic 08 karst caves; Topic 09 lakes and wetlands; Topic 10 coastal erosion. **Topic 04 owns in-situ breakdown, slope transfer and groundwater.**"* ⚠️ **Soil** is owned in addition, because the core owner's section 9 holds it in full. ❗ Within the disaster clause, this file may teach **what fails, why it fails, where in India it fails, what the physical evidence is, and what physical and land-use measures reduce failure**; it may **not** teach **which authority does what, under which strategy, programme, scheme or statute, with which funding line**. ❗ **The test applied to every sentence: if removing the institution's name leaves a physical statement intact, it belongs here; if it leaves nothing, it belongs to another owner.**
- **Typing.** ✅ marks a statement carried in a named source — a canonical owner, a book page listed below, or an official instrument. ⚠️ marks analytical inference, evaluation, standard synthesis or a flagged limitation. 📰 marks a dated current or institutional item. 🔁 marks an ownership routing. ❗ marks a hard prohibition or a discipline an examiner can check. ❌ marks an error being corrected. 🇮🇳 marks an India-specific consequence.
- **Page conventions, recorded so that no citation here is mistaken for another edition's pagination.** **Majid Husain, *Indian and World Geography***, uses **chapter-relative folios** of the form `<chapter>.<page>`; it is the **one text-searchable local volume** and is cited by printed folio. ❗ **G.C. Leong's local scan has a non-uniform printed-to-scan offset**, so Leong is cited **by local-scan page**, and a printed folio or figure number is given **only where directly visible on the rendered page**. ❗ **D.R. Khullar's local scan carries two printed pages per scan page and its offset is NOT uniform across the book** — one offset holds through the earlier span and a different one by the Water Resources chapter — so ❗ **no Khullar folio has been computed anywhere in this file; every folio cited was read off a rendered page**. ❗ Khullar's contents page numbers the hazards chapter differently from the running figures and tables inside it, so ❗ **Khullar is cited by printed page and never by chapter number**. **Majid Husain, *Geography of India***, is a **non-contiguous preview extract** in which roughly one printed page in five survives; ❗ **it is cited here only on printed 6.7, 6.12 and 6.17**, which were directly read.
- **Pages actually read, which bound quotation-grade fidelity.** Majid *Indian and World Geography*: printed **2.22-2.24, 2.41, 5.6-5.11, 10.34** (local-scan pp.44-47, 63, 150-155, 366-368), by text extraction. G.C. Leong: local-scan pp.**30-41**, the complete Chapter 4 and its bounding pages, rendered and read visually. D.R. Khullar: printed **196-197, 202-209, 212-217, 268-273, 314, 560-567, 572-573, 580-583**, rendered and read visually. Majid *Geography of India*: printed **6.7, 6.12, 6.17** plus the contents pages, rendered and read visually. ❗ **No page outside this list is cited anywhere in this file.**
- **Answer-neutrality.** ❗ **No answer letter, truth marking, elimination cue or corrective option mapping appears for any objective demand**, including the two whose official Set-A keys are physically held in the repository. ❗ **No solved previous-year model answer appears**, and no replacement solved-practice workbook has been created.
- **Vector store.** The local vector database was **unreachable** during this pass. It is **optional and non-blocking** under the governing source rule, is **not a source-manifest category**, and this edition does not depend on it and was not delayed by it.

## Corrections and normalisations applied to source material, declared

| # | Source wording or content | Correction applied |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | **Solution defined as "the change of limestone from the solid state to the liquid state by combination with water"** | ❗ **The core owner corrects this by name and the correction is taught as Lesson 3's misconception note.** Carbonation and solution **remove limestone in solution as bicarbonate ions; the rock does not melt.** The physical test — evaporate it; a melt resolidifies, a solution precipitates — is supplied |
| 2 | **Three chemical weathering processes** given in one book — solution, oxidation, decomposition by organic acids | ❗ **Four are taught** — oxidation, carbonation, solution, hydrolysis. The three-fold scheme is named as an **older and narrower classification**, not as an error |
| 3 | **Hydration classified as a physical weathering process** in one book | ❗ The owner and modern treatments class it as **chemical**; the owner controls. **The dispute is disclosed in one sentence** rather than suppressed |
| 4 | **Water said to expand by "one-tenth its volume" on freezing, exerting about 140 kg per sq cm (2,000 lb per sq in)** | ❗ **The owner's "about 9 per cent" controls.** The pressure figure is quoted **only as a dated mid-century order-of-magnitude anchor with the expansion correction attached** |
| 5 | **"Solifluction" used for the whole soil-flow family** in both page-rendered books | ❗ **Corrected explicitly and taught as Lesson 11's misconception note.** Modern usage restricts it to a **periglacial** process — saturated regolith flowing over **frozen ground** — so in India it is a high-Himalayan phenomenon; **debris flow** and **mud flow** are the correct terms for warm-wet cases |
| 6 | **The Soil Survey Division of India's seven-type list with Table 10.17 (Chatterji, 1973 and 1986)** | ❗ **The owner's eight groups control.** The seven-fold list is named as a **superseded alternative classification with its date**, not silently dropped |
| 7 | **"About 21 million hectares" and "about 45 million hectares"** given for severe wind erosion two paragraphs apart on one printed page; plus the surrounding national aggregates and the ICAR line with incoherent units | ❗ **Excluded in full and the contradiction stated.** The owner's caution against quoting national soil-loss rates and degraded-land percentages is converted into a named exclusion, and **structural facts are used in their place** |
| 8 | **Worked groundwater arithmetic that does not close** — a subtraction, a percentage of a stated total, and a "net recharge as 90 per cent of the gross" each inconsistent with the figures given | ❗ **Not reproduced and not reconstructed.** The **budget structure** and the **units problem — million hectare metres against billion cubic metres** — are taught instead, as a method lesson |
| 9 | **Book-era groundwater data** — a 1992-sourced basin table and a 2009 level of development of 61 per cent | ❗ **Both dated and set against the current official assessment, with both dates stated and the gap taught as a method point.** ❗ **The 2009 and 2025 figures are explicitly declared incommensurable and are never differenced** |
| 10 | **Chapter numbering** — one book's contents list the hazards chapter under one number while its running figures and tables use another | ❗ **Cited by printed page, never by chapter number** |
| 11 | **A non-uniform printed-to-scan page offset** in the two-pages-per-scan-page book | ❗ **No folio computed anywhere.** Every folio cited in this file was **read off a rendered page** |
| 12 | **A non-uniform printed-to-scan offset** in a second book, with figure numbering that appears to merge edition sections | ❗ **Cited by local-scan page**; a printed folio or figure number is given **only where visible on the rendered page** |
| 13 | **Desert-soil area given as 1.42 lakh sq km / 4.32 per cent** in one book and **about 15 million hectares / 4.42 per cent** in another | ❗ **Both disclosed with both sources. Not averaged; neither silently preferred.** Since **1.42 lakh sq km = 14.2 million hectares**, both the percentages and absolute areas differ modestly on unstated reporting bases |
| 14 | **An inherited conclusion that the preview-extract book was unusable** | ❗ **Corrected.** The coverage statement is confirmed — roughly one printed page in five survives — **but the usability verdict is reversed for this topic**, because the surviving pages include **printed 6.7, 6.12 and 6.17**, which carry the regur parent-rock passage, the Chambal gully-erosion map and an independent restatement of the erosion-to-groundwater cascade. The source-manifest row records the book as **checked** |

## Open uncertainties carried forward

1. ❗ **Three of the four local books carry no usable text layer, and no optical-character-recognition engine is available in this environment.** Only one volume is text-searchable; the others were recovered by **rendering pages to image and reading them visually**, so **quotation-grade fidelity exists only for the pages enumerated above**. ❗ Pages not rendered were not read, and this file therefore **never asserts that a book is silent** on a point.
2. ❗ **Table 7.1 Area Under Ravines carries no visible source line** on its printed page. The neighbouring attributions on the same page are a 1971 Ministry of Home Affairs working group and a Chambal Development Scheme aerial survey, ❗ **but the table is reproduced here without being attributed to either**, because the page does not do so.
3. ❗ **Two rows of one book's soils-of-India table are garbled** in the available text layer, although the table's percentages sum to 100.00. ❗ **Those rows are not quoted**; the table is used only for its structure.
4. ❗ **A widely cited harvesting claim states a per-person volume with no period attached** on the page. ❗ **It is therefore not quantified anywhere in this file**, and is cited qualitatively or omitted.
5. ❗ **A runoff coefficient of about 0.8 is an inference of this edition**, recovered from the internal arithmetic of a Central Ground Water Board table that **states no coefficient**. ❗ It is **tagged as an inference with its derivation shown wherever it appears**, and must never be attributed to the issuing body.
6. ❗ **Official current-affairs bodies could not be fetched in this environment.** The Central Ground Water Board compilation page returned a transport-level failure and the ministry's findings document returned HTTP 403; the landslide-mapping, remote-sensing and state-disaster-authority pages returned JavaScript-rendered bodies. ❗ **Existence, issuer, title and year were confirmed first-party for all four anchors, and every figure was re-verified at the time of writing against reporting that cites first-party releases.** ❗ **Every such figure carries its issuing body, document title and year in the sentence that uses it; anything that could not be corroborated at the time of writing was dropped rather than estimated.**
7. ❗ **Answer keys.** The 2021 and 2023 objective keys are **unavailable locally**; the **2024 and 2025 Set-A keys are physically present in the repository and are deliberately neither recorded nor inferred**. Answer-neutrality makes this moot for the edition, but it is recorded here.
8. ⚠️ **A book-era procedural claim — that basin-wise groundwater computation ceased in favour of state and union-territory reporting — could not be confirmed first-party** in this environment. ❗ It is presented as **that book's statement with its own vintage**, not as a description of current practice.
9. ⚠️ **The reported fall in the over-exploited share of assessment units between two assessment years** is given as reported, with its attribution as reported. ❗ **This edition does not adjudicate it**, and notes that a change in the number or definition of assessment units can move a category share without any change in the aquifers — the same caution applied consistently to the two national percentages.
10. ⚠️ **No source available to this edition quantifies any of the three structural couplings** on which the file's integration rests. The weathered mantle is **stated** to be the reservoir; erosion is **stated** to lower the water table and increase landslide incidence; bunding is **stated** to have increased well recharge and afforestation to increase seepage. ❗ **Direction is documented; magnitude is not.** The argument is therefore made **structurally**, and attaching a number to any coupling would be an invention.
11. ⚠️ **No measured Indian value exists in this file for weathering rate, regolith thickness, cohesion, angle of internal friction, factor of safety, spring discharge, aquifer yield, intertrappean extent or recharge volume**, because none is carried by any available source. ❗ The one worked stability calculation is **explicitly illustrative**, and each absence is stated at the point where the figure would otherwise have been used.
12. ⚠️ **The four landslide vulnerability zones are a qualitative classification with no stated methodology, thresholds or compilation date** in the source, while the current susceptibility mapping has a stated scale and inventory but **could not be read first-party**. ❗ Both limitations are stated at the point of use, and **neither map is presented as authoritative for any individual slope**.
13. ⚠️ **Lane state.** This edition generates its own complete final arc and source manifest. It makes **no edit to the lane index, to the Geography Mains generation rules, to the canonical owners, to the syllabus-mapping file or to any adjacent lane**, and asserts nothing about them beyond their existence as conventions it follows. ❗ **The syllabus-mapping omission recorded in the coverage matrix is reported as an observation and has not been fixed.**
